**15** Such to you are those with whom you have laboured, who have done business with you from your youth. They wander about, each in his own direction. There is no one to save you.

### Chapter 48

**1** Hear this, house of Jacob, who are called by the name of Israel, and who came from the waters of Judah, who swear by the name of the LORD and confess the God of Israel, but not in truth or right.

**2** For they call themselves after the holy \[H-QADOSH\] city and stay themselves on the God of Israel. The LORD of hosts is his name.

**3** "The former things I declared of old. They went out from my mouth, and I announced them. Then suddenly I did them, and they came to pass.

**4** Because I knew that you are obstinate, and your neck is an iron sinew and your forehead brass,

**5** I declared them to you from of old. Before they came to pass I announced them to you, lest you should say, 'My idol did them, my carved image and my metal image commanded them.'

**6** You have heard. Now see all this. And will you not declare it? From this time forth I announce to you new things, hidden things that you have not known.

**7** They are created now, not long ago. Before today you have never heard of them, lest you should say, 'See, I knew them.'

**8** You have never heard. You have never known. From of old your ear has not been opened. For I knew that you would deal very treacherously, and that from before birth you were called a rebel.

**9** For my name's sake I defer my anger. For the sake of my praise I restrain it for you, that I may not cut you off.

**10** See, I have refined you, but not as silver. I have tried you in the furnace of affliction.

**11** For my own sake, for my own sake, I do it, for how should my name be profaned? My glory I will not give to another.

**12** Listen to me, Jacob, and Israel, whom I called. I am he. I am the first, and I am the last.

**13** My hand laid the foundation of the earth, and my right hand spread out the heavens. When I call to them, they stand forth together.

**14** Assemble, all of you, and listen. Who among them has declared these things? The LORD loves him. He will perform his purpose on Babylon, and his arm will be against the Chaldeans.

**15** I, even I, have spoken and called him. I have brought him, and he will prosper in his way.

**16** Draw near to me, hear this. From the beginning I have not spoken in secret. From the time it came to be I have been there. And now the Lord GOD has sent me, and his Spirit \[H-RUACH\]."

**17** Thus says the LORD, your Redeemer \[H-GOEL\], the Holy One of Israel \[H-QADOSH\]: "I am the LORD your God, who teaches you to profit, who leads you in the way you should go.

**18** Oh that you had paid attention to my commandments. Then your peace \[H-SHALOM\] would have been like a river, and your righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] like the waves of the sea.

**19** Your offspring would have been like the sand, and your descendants like its grains. Their name would never be cut off or destroyed from before me."

**20** Go out from Babylon. Flee from Chaldea. Declare this with a shout of joy. Proclaim it. Send it out to the end of the earth. Say, "The LORD has redeemed his servant Jacob."

**21** They did not thirst when he led them through the deserts. He made water flow for them from the rock. He split the rock, and the water gushed out.

**22** "There is no peace \[H-SHALOM\]," says the LORD, "for the wicked."

*Review note: Isaiah 40 to 48 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the first part of the "comfort of Israel" (chapters 40 to 55), with the call to comfort and the voice in the wilderness (40:1-11), the greatness of the LORD and the folly of idols (40:12-31), the LORD's case with the nations and the assurance to Israel (41:1-29), the first servant song (42:1-9), the new song and the blind servant (42:10-25), the redeemer and the witnesses (43:1-28), the Spirit poured out and the folly of idols (44:1-23), Cyrus and the LORD who made the earth (44:24 to 45:25), the gods of Babylon (46:1-13), the fall of Babylon (47:1-15) and the call to go out from Babylon (48:1-22). In 40:1 "comfort" is tagged H-NACHAM, and the word is the first of the section (*nachamu nachamu*). In 40:7, 40:13, 42:1, 42:5, 44:3 and 48:16 the word *ruach* is tagged H-RUACH. In 40:25, *41:14, 41:16, 43:3*, 43:14, 43:15, 45:11, *47:4, 48:2, 48:17 and 43:28 the title* "the Holy One of Israel" and related words are tagged H-QADOSH. In 41:14, 43:1, 43:14, 44:6, 44:22, 44:24, 47:4 and 48:17 "redeemer" and "redeemed" are tagged H-GOEL. In 42:1 "my soul" is tagged H-NEPHESH, and in 43:4 "your life" is tagged H-NEPHESH. In 42:6 "covenant" is tagged H-BERIT, and in 42:4 and 42:21 "teaching" is tagged H-TORAH. In 43:3 "ransom" is tagged H-KAPPER, and in 47:11 "atone for" is tagged H-KAPPER. In 45:1 "his anointed" translates *li-meshicho*, with the tag H-MASHIACH, and the footnote records that the word is used here of a foreign king, Cyrus. In 45:7 "I make well-being and create calamity" is rendered as written, with no softening, and the footnote records that the Hebrew has *shalom* (with the tag H-SHALOM) and *ra* ("evil" or "calamity"). In 48:18 and 48:22 "peace" is tagged H-SHALOM. In 41:2 "victory" translates *tsedeq* and is tagged H-TSEDAQAH, and the footnote records the readings ("victory", "righteousness"). In 42:10-12 and 43:16-21 the verses are as in the first servant song and the new exodus. In 42:19 "my dedicated one" translates *meshullam*, which is hard. In 43:25 the verse "I, I am he who blots out your transgressions" is rendered as written. In 44:24 "by myself" follows the reading tradition, and the written text has a different form, and the footnote records the matter. In 48:11 the WABV text gives "how should my name be profaned", where the Hebrew has "how should it be profaned", and "my name" is supplied from the Dead Sea scroll and the versions, and it departs from the Hebrew. It is the one departure in the section, and it is flagged for the scholar. The Hebrew is very hard in some places, including 40:12, 40:19-20, 41:2, 41:5-7, 41:24, 42:19-22, 43:3-4, 43:14, 43:23-24, 44:12-13, 44:24, 45:1-2, 45:9, 45:14, 46:1-2, 46:11, 47:1-3, 47:11-15, 48:1, 48:11 and 48:16. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Part Eight: The Servant and the New Zion (Chapters 49 to 55)

### Chapter 49

**1** Listen to me, coastlands, and give attention, you peoples from afar. The LORD called me from the womb. From the body of my mother he named my name.

**2** He made my mouth like a sharp sword. In the shadow of his hand he hid me. He made me a polished arrow. In his quiver he hid me away.

**3** And he said to me, "You are my servant, Israel, in whom I will be glorified."

**4** But I said, "I have laboured in vain. I have spent my strength for nothing and vanity. Yet surely my right is with the LORD, and my recompense with my God."

**5** And now the LORD says, he who formed me from the womb to be his servant, to bring Jacob back to him, and that Israel might be gathered to him, for I am honoured in the eyes of the LORD, and my God has become my strength,

**6** he says: "It is too light a thing that you should be my servant to raise up the tribes of Jacob and to bring back the preserved of Israel. I will make you as a light for the nations, that my salvation may reach to the end of the earth."

**7** Thus says the LORD, the Redeemer \[H-GOEL\] of Israel and his Holy One \[H-QADOSH\], to one deeply despised, abhorred by the nation, the servant of rulers: "Kings will see and arise, princes, and they will prostrate themselves, because of the LORD, who is faithful, the Holy One of Israel, who has chosen you."

**8** Thus says the LORD: "In a time of favour I have answered you. In a day of salvation I have helped you. I will keep you and give you as a covenant \[H-BERIT\] to the people, to establish the land, to apportion the desolate heritages,

**9** saying to the prisoners, 'Come out,' to those who are in darkness, 'Appear.' They will feed along the ways. On all bare heights will be their pasture.

**10** They will not hunger or thirst, neither scorching wind nor sun will strike them, for the one who has pity on them will lead them and by springs of water will guide them.

**11** And I will make all my mountains a road, and my highways will be raised up.

**12** See, these will come from afar, and see, these from the north and from the west, and these from the land of Sinim."

**13** Sing for joy, heavens, and exult, earth. Break forth, mountains, into singing. For the LORD has comforted \[H-NACHAM\] his people and will have compassion on his afflicted.

**14** But Zion said, "The LORD has forsaken me. My Lord has forgotten me."

**15** "Can a woman forget her nursing child, that she should have no compassion on the son of her womb? Even these may forget, yet I will not forget you.

**16** See, I have engraved you on the palms of my hands. Your walls are continually before me.

**17** Your builders make haste. Your destroyers and those who laid you waste go out from you.

**18** Lift up your eyes around and see. They all gather, they come to you. As I live, declares the LORD, you will put them all on as an ornament. You will bind them on as a bride does.

**19** Surely your waste and your desolate places and your devastated land, surely now you will be too narrow for your inhabitants, and those who swallowed you up will be far away.

**20** The children of your bereavement will yet say in your ears: 'The place is too narrow for me. Make room for me to live in.'

**21** Then you will say in your heart: 'Who has borne me these? I was bereaved and barren, exiled and put away, so who has brought up these? See, I was left alone. From where have these come?'"

**22** Thus says the Lord GOD: "See, I will lift up my hand to the nations and raise my signal to the peoples. And they will bring your sons in their arms, and your daughters will be carried on their shoulders.

**23** Kings will be your foster fathers, and their queens your nursing mothers. With their faces to the ground they will bow down to you and lick the dust of your feet. Then you will know that I am the LORD. Those who wait for me will not be put to shame."

**24** Can the prey be taken from the mighty, or the captives of a tyrant be rescued?

**25** Surely, thus says the LORD: "Even the captives of the mighty will be taken, and the prey of the tyrant be rescued, for I will contend with those who contend with you, and I will save your children.

**26** I will make your oppressors eat their own flesh, and they will be drunk with their own blood as with wine. Then all flesh will know that I am the LORD your Saviour and your Redeemer \[H-GOEL\], the Mighty One of Jacob."

### Chapter 50

**1** Thus says the LORD: "Where is your mother's certificate of divorce, with which I sent her away? Or which of my creditors is it to whom I have sold you? See, for your iniquities you were sold, and for your transgressions your mother was sent away.

**2** Why, when I came, was there no one? Why, when I called, was there no one to answer? Is my hand shortened, that it cannot redeem? Or have I no power to deliver? See, by my rebuke I dry up the sea. I make the rivers a desert. Their fish stink for lack of water and die of thirst.

**3** I clothe the heavens with blackness and make sackcloth their covering."

**4** The Lord GOD has given me the tongue of those who are taught, that I may know how to sustain with a word the one who is weary. Morning by morning he awakens. He awakens my ear to hear as those who are taught.

**5** The Lord GOD has opened my ear, and I was not rebellious. I did not turn backward.

**6** I gave my back to those who strike, and my cheeks to those who pull out the beard. I did not hide my face from disgrace and spitting.

**7** But the Lord GOD helps me. Therefore I have not been disgraced. Therefore I have set my face like a flint, and I know that I will not be put to shame.

**8** The one who vindicates me is near. Who will contend with me? Let us stand up together. Who is my adversary? Let him come near to me.

**9** See, the Lord GOD helps me. Who will declare me guilty? See, all of them will wear out like a garment. The moth will eat them up.

**10** Who among you fears the LORD and obeys the voice of his servant? Let the one who walks in darkness and has no light trust in the name of the LORD and rely on his God.

**11** See, all you who kindle a fire, who equip yourselves with burning torches. Walk by the light of your fire, and by the torches that you have kindled. This you have from my hand: you will lie down in torment.

### Chapter 51

**1** "Listen to me, you who pursue righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\], you who seek the LORD. Look to the rock from which you were hewn, and to the quarry from which you were dug.

**2** Look to Abraham your father and to Sarah who bore you. For he was but one when I called him, that I might bless him and multiply him."

**3** For the LORD comforts \[H-NACHAM\] Zion. He comforts all her waste places and makes her wilderness like Eden, her desert like the garden of the LORD. Joy and gladness will be found in her, thanksgiving and the voice of song.

**4** "Give attention to me, my people, and give ear to me, my nation, for a teaching \[H-TORAH\] will go out from me, and I will set my justice for a light to the peoples.

**5** My righteousness draws near. My salvation has gone out. And my arms will judge the peoples. The coastlands hope for me, and for my arm they wait.

**6** Lift up your eyes to the heavens, and look at the earth beneath. For the heavens will vanish like smoke, the earth will wear out like a garment, and those who live in it will die in like manner. But my salvation will be for ever, and my righteousness will never be dismayed.

**7** Listen to me, you who know righteousness, the people in whose heart is my teaching \[H-TORAH\]. Do not fear the reproach of people, nor be dismayed at their revilings.

**8** For the moth will eat them up like a garment, and the worm will eat them like wool. But my righteousness will be for ever, and my salvation to all generations."

**9** Awake, awake, put on strength, arm of the LORD. Awake, as in days of old, the generations of long ago. Was it not you who cut Rahab in pieces, who pierced the dragon?

**10** Was it not you who dried up the sea, the waters of the great deep, who made the depths of the sea a way for the redeemed \[H-GOEL\] to pass over?

**11** And the ransomed of the LORD will return \[H-SHUV\] and come to Zion with singing. Everlasting joy will be on their heads. They will obtain gladness and joy, and sorrow and sighing will flee away.

**12** "I, I am he who comforts \[H-NACHAM\] you. Who are you that you are afraid of a mortal who dies, of a human being who is made like grass,

**13** and have forgotten the LORD, your Maker, who stretched out the heavens and laid the foundations of the earth, and you fear continually all the day because of the wrath of the oppressor, when he sets himself to destroy? And where is the wrath of the oppressor?

**14** The one who is bowed down will speedily be released. He will not die and go down to the pit, neither will his bread be lacking.

**15** I am the LORD your God, who stirs up the sea so that its waves roar. The LORD of hosts is his name.

**16** And I have put my words in your mouth and covered you in the shadow of my hand, establishing the heavens and laying the foundations of the earth, and saying to Zion, 'You are my people.'"

**17** Wake yourself, wake yourself, stand up, Jerusalem, you who have drunk from the hand of the LORD the cup of his wrath, who have drunk to the dregs the bowl, the cup of staggering.

**18** There is no one to guide her among all the sons she has borne. There is no one to take her by the hand among all the sons she has brought up.

**19** These two things have happened to you. Who will console \[H-NACHAM\] you? Devastation and destruction, famine and sword. Who will comfort you?

**20** Your sons have fainted. They lie at the head of every street like an antelope in a net. They are full of the wrath of the LORD, the rebuke of your God.

**21** Therefore hear this, you who are afflicted, who are drunk, but not with wine.

**22** Thus says your Lord, the LORD, your God who pleads the cause of his people: "See, I have taken from your hand the cup of staggering. You will drink no more from the bowl of my wrath.

**23** And I will put it into the hand of your tormentors, who have said to you, 'Bow down, that we may pass over.' And you have made your back like the ground and like the street for them to pass over."

### Chapter 52

**1** Awake, awake, put on your strength, Zion. Put on your beautiful garments, Jerusalem, the holy \[H-QADOSH\] city, for there will no more come into you the uncircumcised and the unclean.

**2** Shake yourself from the dust and arise. Be seated, Jerusalem. Loose the bonds from your neck, captive daughter of Zion.

**3** For thus says the LORD: "You were sold for nothing, and you will be redeemed without money."

**4** For thus says the Lord GOD: "My people went down at the first into Egypt to live there, and the Assyrian oppressed them for nothing.

**5** Now therefore what have I here," declares the LORD, "seeing that my people are taken away for nothing? Their rulers wail," declares the LORD, "and continually all the day my name is despised.

**6** Therefore my people will know my name. Therefore in that day they will know that it is I who speak. Here I am."

**7** How beautiful on the mountains are the feet of the one who brings good news, who publishes peace \[H-SHALOM\], who brings good news of happiness, who publishes salvation, who says to Zion, "Your God reigns."

**8** The voice of your watchmen. They lift up their voice. Together they sing for joy, for eye to eye they see the return of the LORD to Zion.

**9** Break forth together into singing, you waste places of Jerusalem, for the LORD has comforted \[H-NACHAM\] his people. He has redeemed \[H-GOEL\] Jerusalem.

**10** The LORD has bared his holy \[H-QADOSH\] arm before the eyes of all the nations, and all the ends of the earth will see the salvation of our God.

**11** Depart, depart, go out from there. Touch no unclean thing. Go out from the midst of her. Purify yourselves, you who bear the vessels of the LORD.

**12** For you will not go out in haste, and you will not go in flight, for the LORD will go before you, and the God of Israel will be your rear guard.

**13** See, my servant will act wisely. He will be high and lifted up, and will be exalted.

**14** As many were astonished at you, his appearance was so marred, beyond human semblance, and his form beyond that of humankind,

**15** so he will sprinkle many nations. Kings will shut their mouths because of him. For that which has not been told them they see, and that which they have not heard they understand.

### Chapter 53

**1** Who has believed what he has heard from us? And to whom has the arm of the LORD been revealed?

**2** For he grew up before him like a young plant, and like a root out of dry ground. He had no form or majesty that we should look at him, and no beauty that we should desire him.

**3** He was despised and rejected by people, a man of pains and familiar with sickness. Like one from whom people hide their faces, he was despised, and we did not value him.

**4** Surely he has borne our sicknesses and carried our pains. Yet we considered him struck, beaten by God, and humbled.

**5** But he was pierced for our transgressions. He was crushed for our iniquities. The discipline that brought us peace \[H-SHALOM\] was on him, and by his wounds we are healed.

**6** All of us like sheep have gone astray. Each has turned to his own way. And the LORD has laid on him the iniquity of us all.

**7** He was oppressed, and he was afflicted, yet he did not open his mouth. Like a lamb that is led to the slaughter, and like a sheep that before its shearers is silent, so he did not open his mouth.

**8** By restraint and judgement he was taken away. And as for his generation, who considered that he was cut off from the land of the living, struck for the transgression of my people?

**9** And his grave was assigned with the wicked, and with a rich man in his death, although he had done no violence and there was no deceit in his mouth.

**10** Yet it was the LORD's will to crush him. He has put him to grief. When his soul \[H-NEPHESH\] makes a guilt offering, he will see offspring. He will prolong his days, and the LORD's purpose will prosper in his hand.

**11** Out of the anguish of his soul he will see and be satisfied. By his knowledge the righteous one, my servant, will make many to be accounted righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\], and he will bear their iniquities.

**12** Therefore I will give him a portion with the many, and he will divide the spoil with the strong, because he poured out his soul \[H-NEPHESH\] to death and was counted with the transgressors. Yet he bore the sin of many and interceded for the transgressors.

### Chapter 54

**1** "Sing, barren one, who did not bear. Break forth into singing and cry aloud, you who have not been in labour. For the children of the desolate one will be more than the children of her who is married," says the LORD.

**2** "Enlarge the place of your tent, and let the curtains of your habitations be stretched out. Do not hold back. Lengthen your cords and strengthen your stakes.

**3** For you will spread abroad to the right and to the left, and your offspring will possess the nations and will people the desolate cities.

**4** Do not fear, for you will not be ashamed. Do not be confounded, for you will not be disgraced. For you will forget the shame of your youth, and the reproach of your widowhood you will remember no more.

**5** For your Maker is your husband. The LORD of hosts is his name. And the Holy One of Israel \[H-QADOSH\] is your Redeemer \[H-GOEL\]. The God of the whole earth he is called.

**6** For the LORD has called you like a wife deserted and grieved in spirit \[H-RUACH\], like a wife of youth when she is cast off, says your God.

**7** For a brief moment I deserted you, but with great compassion I will gather you.

**8** In overflowing anger for a moment I hid my face from you, but with everlasting steadfast love \[H-HESED\] I will have compassion on you," says the LORD, your Redeemer.

**9** "This is like the days of Noah to me. As I swore that the waters of Noah should no more go over the earth, so I have sworn that I will not be angry with you and will not rebuke you.

**10** For the mountains may depart and the hills be removed, but my steadfast love \[H-HESED\] will not depart from you, and my covenant \[H-BERIT\] of peace \[H-SHALOM\] will not be removed," says the LORD, who has compassion on you.

**11** "Afflicted one, storm-tossed and not comforted \[H-NACHAM\], see, I will set your stones in antimony and lay your foundations with sapphires.

**12** I will make your pinnacles of agate, your gates of carbuncles, and all your wall of precious stones.

**13** All your children will be taught by the LORD, and great will be the peace \[H-SHALOM\] of your children.

**14** In righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] you will be established. You will be far from oppression, for you will not fear, and from terror, for it will not come near you.

**15** If anyone stirs up strife, it is not from me. Whoever stirs up strife with you will fall because of you.

**16** See, I have created the smith who blows the fire of coals and produces a weapon for its purpose. I have also created the ravager to destroy.

**17** No weapon that is fashioned against you will succeed, and you will refute every tongue that rises against you in judgement. This is the heritage of the servants of the LORD and their vindication from me," declares the LORD.

### Chapter 55

**1** "Come, all who are thirsty, come to the waters. And you who have no money, come, buy and eat. Come, buy wine and milk without money and without price.

**2** Why do you spend your money for that which is not bread, and your labour for that which does not satisfy? Listen diligently to me, and eat what is good, and delight yourselves in rich food.

**3** Incline your ear, and come to me. Hear, that your soul \[H-NEPHESH\] may live. And I will make with you an everlasting covenant \[H-BERIT\], the steadfast, sure love \[H-HESED\] shown to David.

**4** See, I made him a witness to the peoples, a leader and commander for the peoples.

**5** See, you will call a nation that you do not know, and a nation that did not know you will run to you, because of the LORD your God, and of the Holy One of Israel \[H-QADOSH\], for he has glorified you."

**6** Seek the LORD while he may be found. Call on him while he is near.

**7** Let the wicked forsake his way, and the unrighteous his thoughts. Let him return \[H-SHUV\] to the LORD, that he may have compassion on him, and to our God, for he will abundantly pardon.

**8** For my thoughts are not your thoughts, neither are your ways my ways, declares the LORD.

**9** For as the heavens are higher than the earth, so are my ways higher than your ways and my thoughts than your thoughts.

**10** For as the rain and the snow come down from heaven and do not return \[H-SHUV\] there but water the earth, making it bring forth and sprout, giving seed to the sower and bread to the eater,

**11** so will my word be that goes out from my mouth. It will not return to me empty, but it will accomplish that which I purpose, and succeed in the thing for which I sent it.

**12** For you will go out in joy and be led forth in peace \[H-SHALOM\]. The mountains and the hills before you will break forth into singing, and all the trees of the field will clap their hands.

**13** Instead of the thorn will come up the cypress. Instead of the brier will come up the myrtle. And it will make a name for the LORD, an everlasting sign that will not be cut off."

*Review note: Isaiah 49 to 55 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the second part of the "comfort of Israel", with the second servant song (49:1-6), the promises to the servant and to Zion (49:7-26), the third servant song and the call to the faithful (50:1-11), the call to listen and to awake (51:1-23), the good news on the mountains and the call to depart from Babylon (52:1-12), the fourth servant song (52:13 to 53:12), the barren woman who sings (54:1-17) and the invitation to the thirsty (55:1-13). In 49:7, 49:26, 52:9, 54:5 and 54:8 "Redeemer" and "redeemed" are tagged H-GOEL, and in 49:13, 51:3, 51:12, 51:19, 52:9 and 54:11 "comfort" is tagged H-NACHAM. In 49:8 and 54:10 and 55:3 "covenant" is tagged H-BERIT. In 49:7, 52:1, 52:10, 54:5 and 55:5 "holy" and "the Holy One of Israel" are tagged H-QADOSH. In 51:4 and 51:7 "teaching" is tagged H-TORAH. In 52:7, 53:5, 54:10, 54:13 and 55:12 "peace" is tagged H-SHALOM. In 53:10, 53:12 and 55:3 "soul" is tagged H-NEPHESH. In 53:11, 51:1 and 54:14 "righteous" and "righteousness" are tagged H-TSEDAQAH. In 54:6 "spirit" is tagged H-RUACH. In 54:8, 54:10 and 55:3 "steadfast love" is tagged H-HESED. In 51:11 the wording is the same as 35:10, and in 51:10 "the redeemed" is the same as 35:9, and the three are matched. In 51:11 and 55:7, 55:10 "return" is tagged H-SHUV. In 52:13 to 53:12 the fourth servant song is rendered as written, with no comment on who the servant is, and the footnotes record the language, the uncertain Hebrew and the places where the New Testament quotes the passage (Matthew 8:17, Luke 22:37, John 12:38, Acts 8:32-33, Romans 10:16, 1 Peter 2:22-25). In 53:5 "pierced" translates *mecholal*, and "the discipline that brought us peace" translates *musar shelomenu*. In 53:8 and 53:9 and 53:10 the Hebrew is very hard. In 53:10 "when his soul makes a guilt offering" translates *im-tasim asham nafsho*, in which the verb may be "you make" or "it makes", and the footnote records the matter. In 53:12 "with the many" translates *ba-rabbim*, which may also be read "among the great". In 49:24 the WABV text gives "the captives of a tyrant", following the Dead Sea scroll, the Syriac and the Latin, where the Hebrew text has "the captives of the righteous" (*tsaddiq*), and it departs from the Hebrew. *At 54:9 the WABV text gives "the days of Noah", following the Dead Sea scroll and the versions (*mei Noach* read as "days"), where the Hebrew text has "the waters of Noah", and it departs from the Hebrew. These are the two departures in the section, and they are flagged for the scholar. In 49:12* "Sinim" is given as the Hebrew has it. In 54:1 and 54:4-6 the barren woman and the wife are figures of Zion. In 55:3 "the steadfast, sure love shown to David" translates *chasdei David ha-ne'emanim*, and the footnote records the readings. The Hebrew is very hard in some places, including 49:5, 49:12, 49:24, 50:4, 50:10, 51:19, 52:14-15, 53:3, 53:8-10, 53:12, 54:15-16 and 55:3. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Part Nine: The Return and the New Creation (Chapters 56 to 66)

### Chapter 56

**1** Thus says the LORD: "Keep justice and do righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\], for soon my salvation will come, and my righteousness be revealed.

**2** Blessed is the one who does this, and the human being who holds it fast, who keeps the Sabbath, not profaning it, and keeps his hand from doing any evil."

**3** Let not the foreigner who has joined himself to the LORD say, "The LORD will surely separate me from his people." And let not the eunuch say, "See, I am a dry tree."

**4** For thus says the LORD: "To the eunuchs who keep my Sabbaths, who choose the things that please me and hold fast my covenant \[H-BERIT\],

**5** I will give in my house and within my walls a monument and a name better than sons and daughters. I will give them an everlasting name that will not be cut off.

**6** And the foreigners who join themselves to the LORD, to minister to him, to love the name of the LORD, and to be his servants, everyone who keeps the Sabbath and does not profane it, and holds fast my covenant,

**7** these I will bring to my holy \[H-QADOSH\] mountain and make them joyful in my house of prayer. Their burnt offerings and their sacrifices will be accepted on my altar, for my house will be called a house of prayer for all peoples."

**8** The Lord GOD, who gathers the outcasts of Israel, declares, "I will gather yet others to him besides those already gathered."

**9** All you beasts of the field, come to devour, all you beasts in the forest.

**10** His watchmen are blind. They are all without knowledge. They are all silent dogs. They cannot bark. Dreaming, lying down, loving to slumber.

**11** The dogs have a mighty appetite. They never have enough. But they are shepherds who have no understanding. They have all turned to their own way, each to his own gain, one and all.

**12** "Come," they say, "let me get wine. Let us fill ourselves with strong drink. And tomorrow will be like this day, great beyond measure."

### Chapter 57

**1** The righteous perish, and no one lays it to heart. The devout are taken away, while no one understands. For the righteous are taken away from calamity.

**2** They enter into peace \[H-SHALOM\]. They rest in their beds who walk in their uprightness.

**3** But you, draw near, sons of the sorceress, offspring of the adulterer and the loose woman.

**4** Whom are you mocking? Against whom do you open your mouth wide and stick out your tongue? Are you not children of transgression, the offspring of deceit,

**5** you who burn with lust among the oaks, under every green tree, who slaughter your children in the valleys, under the clefts of the rocks?

**6** Among the smooth stones of the valley is your portion. They, they are your lot. To them you have poured out a drink offering. You have brought a grain offering. Shall I relent for these things?

**7** On a high and lofty mountain you have set your bed, and there you went up to offer sacrifice.

**8** Behind the door and the doorpost you have set up your memorial. For, deserting me, you have uncovered your bed. You have gone up to it. You have made it wide. And you have made a covenant \[H-BERIT\] for yourself with them. You have loved their bed. You have looked on nakedness.

**9** You journeyed to the king with oil and multiplied your perfumes. You sent your envoys far off, and sent down even to Sheol \[H-SHEOL\].

**10** You were wearied with the length of your way, but you did not say, "It is hopeless." You found new life for your strength, and so you were not faint.

**11** Whom did you dread and fear, so that you lied and did not remember me, did not lay it to heart? Have I not held my peace, even for a long time, and you do not fear me?

**12** I will declare your righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] and your deeds, but they will not help you.

**13** When you cry out, let your collection of idols deliver you. The wind will carry them off. A breath will take them away. But the one who takes refuge in me will possess the land and will inherit my holy \[H-QADOSH\] mountain.

**14** And it will be said, "Build up, build up, prepare the way. Remove every obstruction from my people's way."

**15** For thus says the One who is high and lifted up, who inhabits eternity, whose name is Holy \[H-QADOSH\]: "I live in the high and holy place, and also with the one who is of a contrite and lowly spirit \[H-RUACH\], to revive the spirit of the lowly and to revive the heart of the contrite.

**16** For I will not contend for ever, nor will I always be angry, for the spirit \[H-RUACH\] would grow faint before me, and the breath of life that I made.

**17** Because of the iniquity of his unjust gain I was angry. I struck him. I hid my face and was angry, but he went on backsliding in the way of his own heart.

**18** I have seen his ways, but I will heal him. I will lead him and restore comfort \[H-NACHAM\] to him and his mourners,

**19** creating the fruit of the lips. Peace, peace \[H-SHALOM\], to the far and to the near," says the LORD, "and I will heal him."

**20** But the wicked are like the tossing sea, for it cannot be quiet, and its waters toss up mire and dirt.

**21** "There is no peace \[H-SHALOM\]," says my God, "for the wicked."

### Chapter 58

**1** "Cry aloud. Do not hold back. Lift up your voice like a trumpet. Declare to my people their transgression, to the house of Jacob their sins.

**2** Yet they seek me daily and delight to know my ways, as if they were a nation that did righteousness and did not forsake the judgement of their God. They ask me for righteous judgements. They delight to draw near to God.

**3** 'Why have we fasted, and you do not see it? Why have we humbled ourselves, and you take no knowledge of it?' See, in the day of your fast you seek your own pleasure and oppress all your workers.

**4** See, you fast only to quarrel and to fight and to hit with a wicked fist. Fasting like yours this day will not make your voice to be heard on high.

**5** Is such the fast that I choose, a day for a person to humble himself? Is it to bow down his head like a reed and to spread sackcloth and ashes under him? Will you call this a fast, and a day acceptable to the LORD?

**6** Is not this the fast that I choose: to loose the bonds of wickedness, to undo the straps of the yoke, to let the oppressed go free, and to break every yoke?

**7** Is it not to share your bread with the hungry and bring the homeless poor into your house, when you see the naked, to cover him, and not to hide yourself from your own flesh?

**8** Then your light will break forth like the dawn, and your healing will spring up speedily. Your righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] will go before you. The glory of the LORD will be your rear guard.

**9** Then you will call, and the LORD will answer. You will cry, and he will say, 'Here I am.' If you take away the yoke from your midst, the pointing of the finger, and speaking wickedness,

**10** if you pour yourself out for the hungry and satisfy the desire \[H-NEPHESH\] of the afflicted, then your light will rise in the darkness and your gloom be as the noonday.

**11** And the LORD will guide you continually and satisfy your desire \[H-NEPHESH\] in scorched places and make your bones strong. And you will be like a watered garden, like a spring of water, whose waters do not fail.

**12** And your ancient ruins will be rebuilt. You will raise up the foundations of many generations. You will be called the repairer of the breach, the restorer of streets to live in.

**13** If you turn back your foot from the Sabbath, from doing your pleasure on my holy \[H-QADOSH\] day, and call the Sabbath a delight and the holy day of the LORD honourable, if you honour it, not going your own ways or seeking your own pleasure or talking idly,

**14** then you will take delight in the LORD, and I will make you ride on the heights of the earth. I will feed you with the heritage of Jacob your father, for the mouth of the LORD has spoken."

### Chapter 59

**1** See, the LORD's hand is not shortened, that it cannot save, or his ear dull, that it cannot hear.

**2** But your iniquities have made a separation between you and your God, and your sins have hidden his face from you so that he does not hear.

**3** For your hands are defiled with blood and your fingers with iniquity. Your lips have spoken lies. Your tongue mutters wickedness.

**4** No one enters suit justly. No one goes to law honestly. They rely on empty pleas, they speak lies, they conceive mischief and give birth to iniquity.

**5** They hatch adders' eggs. They weave the spider's web. The one who eats their eggs dies, and from one that is crushed a viper is hatched.

**6** Their webs will not serve as clothing. People will not cover themselves with what they make. Their works are works of iniquity, and deeds of violence are in their hands.

**7** Their feet run to evil, and they are swift to shed innocent blood. Their thoughts are thoughts of iniquity. Desolation and destruction are in their highways.

**8** The way of peace \[H-SHALOM\] they do not know, and there is no justice in their paths. They have made their roads crooked. No one who treads on them knows peace.

**9** Therefore justice is far from us, and righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] does not overtake us. We hope for light, and see, darkness, and for brightness, but we walk in gloom.

**10** We grope for the wall like the blind. We grope like those who have no eyes. We stumble at noon as in the twilight. Among those in full vigour we are like the dead.

**11** We all growl like bears. We moan and moan like doves. We hope for justice, but there is none, for salvation, but it is far from us.

**12** For our transgressions are multiplied before you, and our sins testify against us. For our transgressions are with us, and we know our iniquities:

**13** transgressing, and denying the LORD, and turning back from following our God, speaking oppression and revolt, conceiving and uttering from the heart lying words.

**14** Justice is turned back, and righteousness stands far away. For truth has stumbled in the public squares, and uprightness cannot enter.

**15** Truth is lacking, and the one who departs from evil makes himself a prey. The LORD saw it, and it displeased him that there was no justice.

**16** He saw that there was no one, and wondered that there was no one to intercede. Then his own arm brought him salvation, and his righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] upheld him.

**17** He put on righteousness as a breastplate, and a helmet of salvation on his head. He put on garments of vengeance for clothing and wrapped himself in zeal as a cloak.

**18** According to their deeds, so will he repay, wrath to his adversaries, repayment to his enemies. To the coastlands he will render repayment.

**19** So they will fear the name of the LORD from the west, and his glory from the rising of the sun. For he will come like a rushing stream, which the wind \[H-RUACH\] of the LORD drives.

**20** "And a Redeemer \[H-GOEL\] will come to Zion, to those in Jacob who turn \[H-SHUV\] from transgression," declares the LORD.

**21** "And as for me, this is my covenant \[H-BERIT\] with them," says the LORD: "My Spirit \[H-RUACH\] that is on you, and my words that I have put in your mouth, will not depart out of your mouth, or out of the mouth of your offspring, or out of the mouth of your children's offspring," says the LORD, "from this time forth and for evermore."

### Chapter 60

**1** Arise, shine, for your light has come, and the glory of the LORD has risen on you.

**2** For see, darkness will cover the earth, and thick darkness the peoples. But the LORD will arise on you, and his glory will be seen on you.

**3** And nations will come to your light, and kings to the brightness of your rising.

**4** Lift up your eyes all around, and see. They all gather, they come to you. Your sons will come from afar, and your daughters will be carried on the hip.

**5** Then you will see and be radiant. Your heart will thrill and exult, because the abundance of the sea will be turned to you, the wealth of the nations will come to you.

**6** A multitude of camels will cover you, the young camels of Midian and Ephah. All those from Sheba will come. They will bring gold and frankincense and will proclaim the praise of the LORD.

**7** All the flocks of Kedar will be gathered to you. The rams of Nebaioth will minister to you. They will come up with acceptance on my altar, and I will beautify my beautiful house.

**8** Who are these that fly like a cloud, and like doves to their windows?

**9** For the coastlands will hope for me, the ships of Tarshish first, to bring your children from afar, their silver and gold with them, for the name of the LORD your God, and for the Holy One of Israel \[H-QADOSH\], because he has made you beautiful.

**10** Foreigners will build up your walls, and their kings will minister to you. For in my wrath I struck you, but in my favour I have had mercy on you.

**11** Your gates will be open continually. Day and night they will not be shut, that people may bring to you the wealth of the nations, with their kings led in procession.

**12** For the nation and kingdom that will not serve you will perish. Those nations will be utterly laid waste.

**13** The glory of Lebanon will come to you, the cypress, the plane and the pine together, to beautify the place of my sanctuary, and I will make the place of my feet glorious.

**14** The sons of those who afflicted you will come bending low to you, and all who despised you will bow down at your feet. They will call you the City of the LORD, the Zion of the Holy One of Israel \[H-QADOSH\].

**15** Whereas you have been forsaken and hated, with no one passing through, I will make you majestic for ever, a joy from age to age.

**16** You will suck the milk of nations. You will nurse at the breast of kings. And you will know that I, the LORD, am your Saviour and your Redeemer \[H-GOEL\], the Mighty One of Jacob.

**17** Instead of bronze I will bring gold, and instead of iron I will bring silver, instead of wood, bronze, instead of stones, iron. I will make your overseers peace \[H-SHALOM\] and your taskmasters righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\].

**18** Violence will no more be heard in your land, nor devastation or destruction within your borders. You will call your walls Salvation, and your gates Praise.

**19** The sun will be no more your light by day, nor for brightness will the moon give you light. But the LORD will be your everlasting light, and your God will be your glory.

**20** Your sun will no more go down, nor your moon withdraw itself. For the LORD will be your everlasting light, and your days of mourning will be ended.

**21** Your people will all be righteous \[H-TSEDAQAH\]. They will possess the land for ever, the branch of my planting, the work of my hands, that I might be glorified.

**22** The least one will become a clan, and the smallest one a mighty nation. I am the LORD. In its time I will hasten it.

### Chapter 61

**1** The Spirit \[H-RUACH\] of the Lord GOD is on me, because the LORD has anointed \[H-MASHIACH\] me to bring good news to the poor. He has sent me to bind up the brokenhearted, to proclaim liberty to the captives, and the opening of the prison to those who are bound,

**2** to proclaim the year of the LORD's favour, and the day of vengeance of our God, to comfort \[H-NACHAM\] all who mourn,

**3** to grant to those who mourn in Zion, to give them a beautiful headdress instead of ashes, the oil of gladness instead of mourning, the garment of praise instead of a faint spirit \[H-RUACH\], that they may be called oaks of righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\], the planting of the LORD, that he may be glorified.

**4** They will build up the ancient ruins. They will raise up the former devastations. They will repair the ruined cities, the devastations of many generations.

**5** Strangers will stand and tend your flocks. Foreigners will be your ploughmen and vinedressers.

**6** But you will be called the priests of the LORD. They will speak of you as the ministers of our God. You will eat the wealth of the nations, and in their glory you will boast.

**7** Instead of your shame there will be a double portion. Instead of dishonour they will rejoice in their lot. Therefore in their land they will possess a double portion. They will have everlasting joy.

**8** For I the LORD love justice. I hate robbery and wrong. I will faithfully give them their recompense, and I will make an everlasting covenant \[H-BERIT\] with them.

**9** Their offspring will be known among the nations, and their descendants in the midst of the peoples. All who see them will acknowledge them, that they are an offspring the LORD has blessed.

**10** I will greatly rejoice in the LORD. My soul \[H-NEPHESH\] will exult in my God, for he has clothed me with the garments of salvation. He has covered me with the robe of righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\], as a bridegroom decks himself like a priest with a beautiful headdress, and as a bride adorns herself with her jewels.

**11** For as the earth brings forth its sprouts, and as a garden causes what is sown in it to sprout up, so the Lord GOD will cause righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] and praise to sprout up before all the nations.

### Chapter 62

**1** For Zion's sake I will not keep silent, and for Jerusalem's sake I will not be still, until her righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\] goes forth like brightness, and her salvation like a burning torch.

**2** The nations will see your righteousness, and all the kings your glory, and you will be called by a new name that the mouth of the LORD will give.

**3** You will be a crown of beauty in the hand of the LORD, and a royal diadem in the hand of your God.

**4** You will no more be termed Forsaken, and your land will no more be termed Desolate, but you will be called My Delight Is in Her, and your land Married. For the LORD delights in you, and your land will be married.

**5** For as a young man marries a young woman, so your sons will marry you, and as the bridegroom rejoices over the bride, so your God will rejoice over you.

**6** On your walls, Jerusalem, I have set watchmen. All the day and all the night they will never be silent. You who put the LORD in remembrance, take no rest,

**7** and give him no rest until he establishes Jerusalem and makes it a praise in the earth.

**8** The LORD has sworn by his right hand and by his mighty arm: "I will not again give your grain to be food for your enemies, and foreigners will not drink your wine for which you have laboured,

**9** but those who garner it will eat it and praise the LORD, and those who gather it will drink it in the courts of my sanctuary."

**10** Go through, go through the gates. Prepare the way for the people. Build up, build up the highway. Clear it of stones. Lift up a signal over the peoples.

**11** See, the LORD has proclaimed to the end of the earth: "Say to the daughter of Zion, 'See, your salvation comes. See, his reward is with him, and his recompense before him.'"

**12** And they will be called The Holy \[H-QADOSH\] People, The Redeemed \[H-GOEL\] of the LORD. And you will be called Sought Out, A City Not Forsaken.

### Chapter 63

**1** Who is this who comes from Edom, in crimsoned garments from Bozrah, he who is splendid in his apparel, marching in the greatness of his strength? "It is I, speaking in righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\], mighty to save."

**2** Why is your apparel red, and your garments like those of one who treads in the winepress?

**3** "I have trodden the winepress alone, and from the peoples no one was with me. I trod them in my anger and trampled them in my wrath. Their lifeblood spattered on my garments and stained all my apparel.

**4** For the day of vengeance was in my heart, and my year of redemption \[H-GOEL\] had come.

**5** I looked, but there was no one to help. I was appalled, but there was no one to uphold. So my own arm brought me salvation, and my wrath upheld me.

**6** I trampled down the peoples in my anger. I made them drunk in my wrath, and I poured out their lifeblood on the earth."

**7** I will recount the steadfast love \[H-HESED\] of the LORD, the praises of the LORD, according to all that the LORD has granted us, and the great goodness to the house of Israel that he has granted them according to his compassion, according to the abundance of his steadfast love \[H-HESED\].

**8** For he said, "Surely they are my people, sons who will not deal falsely." And he became their Saviour.

**9** In all their affliction he was afflicted, and the angel of his presence saved them. In his love and in his pity he redeemed \[H-GOEL\] them. He lifted them up and carried them all the days of old.

**10** But they rebelled and grieved his holy \[H-QADOSH\] Spirit \[H-RUACH\]. Therefore he turned to be their enemy, and himself fought against them.

**11** Then he remembered the days of old, of Moses and his people. Where is the one who brought them up out of the sea with the shepherds of his flock? Where is the one who put in the midst of them his holy \[H-QADOSH\] Spirit \[H-RUACH\],

**12** who caused his glorious arm to go at the right hand of Moses, who divided the waters before them to make for himself an everlasting name,

**13** who led them through the depths? Like a horse in the desert, they did not stumble.

**14** Like livestock that go down into the valley, the Spirit \[H-RUACH\] of the LORD gave them rest. So you led your people, to make for yourself a glorious name.

**15** Look down from heaven and see, from your holy \[H-QADOSH\] and beautiful habitation. Where are your zeal and your might? The stirring of your inner parts and your compassion are held back from me.

**16** For you are our Father, though Abraham does not know us, and Israel does not acknowledge us. You, LORD, are our Father. Our Redeemer \[H-GOEL\] from of old is your name.

**17** LORD, why do you make us wander from your ways and harden our heart, so that we do not fear you? Return \[H-SHUV\] for the sake of your servants, for the sake of the tribes of your heritage.

**18** Your holy \[H-QADOSH\] people held possession for a little while. Our adversaries have trodden down your sanctuary.

**19** We have become like those over whom you have never ruled, like those who are not called by your name.

### Chapter 64

**1** Oh that you would rend the heavens and come down, that the mountains might quake at your presence,

**2** as when fire kindles brushwood and the fire causes water to boil, to make your name known to your adversaries, and that the nations might tremble at your presence.

**3** When you did awesome things that we did not look for, you came down. The mountains quaked at your presence.

**4** From of old no one has heard or perceived by the ear, no eye has seen a God besides you, who acts for those who wait for him.

**5** You meet the one who joyfully works righteousness \[H-TSEDAQAH\], those who remember you in your ways. See, you were angry, and we sinned. In our sins we have been a long time, and will we be saved?

**6** We have all become like one who is unclean, and all our righteous deeds are like a polluted garment. We all fade like a leaf, and our iniquities, like the wind, take us away.

**7** There is no one who calls on your name, who rouses himself to take hold of you. For you have hidden your face from us and have made us melt in the hand of our iniquities.

**8** But now, LORD, you are our Father. We are the clay, and you are our potter. We are all the work of your hand.

**9** Do not be so terribly angry, LORD, and do not remember iniquity for ever. See, please look, we are all your people.

**10** Your holy \[H-QADOSH\] cities have become a wilderness. Zion has become a wilderness, Jerusalem a desolation.

**11** Our holy \[H-QADOSH\] and beautiful house, where our fathers praised you, has been burned by fire, and all our pleasant places have become ruins.

**12** Will you restrain yourself at these things, LORD? Will you keep silent and afflict us so severely?

### Chapter 65

**1** "I was ready to be sought by those who did not ask for me. I was ready to be found by those who did not seek me. I said, 'Here I am, here I am,' to a nation that was not called by my name.

**2** I spread out my hands all the day to a rebellious people, who walk in a way that is not good, following their own devices,

**3** a people who provoke me to my face continually, sacrificing in gardens and making offerings on bricks,

**4** who sit in tombs and spend the night in secret places, who eat pig's flesh, and broth of tainted meat is in their vessels,

**5** who say, 'Keep to yourself. Do not come near me, for I am too holy \[H-QADOSH\] for you.' These are a smoke in my nostrils, a fire that burns all the day.

**6** See, it is written before me: 'I will not keep silent, but I will repay. I will indeed repay into their lap

**7** both your iniquities and your fathers' iniquities together,' says the LORD. 'Because they made offerings on the mountains and insulted me on the hills, I will measure into their lap payment for their former deeds.'

**8** Thus says the LORD: 'As the new wine is found in the cluster, and they say, "Do not destroy it, for there is a blessing in it," so will I do for my servants' sake, and not destroy them all.

**9** I will bring forth offspring from Jacob, and from Judah possessors of my mountains. My chosen will possess it, and my servants will live there.

**10** Sharon will become a pasture for flocks, and the Valley of Achor a place for herds to lie down, for my people who have sought me.

**11** But you who forsake the LORD, who forget my holy \[H-QADOSH\] mountain, who set a table for Fortune and fill cups of mixed wine for Destiny,

**12** I will destine you to the sword, and all of you will bow down to the slaughter, because, when I called, you did not answer. When I spoke, you did not listen, but you did what was evil in my eyes and chose what I did not delight in.'

**13** Therefore thus says the Lord GOD: 'See, my servants will eat, but you will be hungry. See, my servants will drink, but you will be thirsty. See, my servants will rejoice, but you will be put to shame.

**14** See, my servants will sing for gladness of heart, but you will cry out for pain of heart and will wail for breaking of spirit \[H-RUACH\].

**15** You will leave your name to my chosen for a curse, and the Lord GOD will put you to death, but his servants he will call by another name.

**16** So that the one who blesses himself in the land will bless himself by the God of truth, and the one who takes an oath in the land will swear by the God of truth, because the former troubles are forgotten and are hidden from my eyes.

**17** For see, I create new heavens and a new earth, and the former things will not be remembered or come into mind.

**18** But be glad and rejoice for ever in that which I create, for see, I create Jerusalem to be a joy, and her people to be a gladness.

**19** I will rejoice in Jerusalem and be glad in my people. No more will be heard in it the sound of weeping and the cry of distress.

**20** No more will there be in it an infant who lives but a few days, or an old person who does not fill out his days, for the young will die a hundred years old, and the sinner a hundred years old will be accursed.

**21** They will build houses and live in them. They will plant vineyards and eat their fruit.

**22** They will not build and another live in them. They will not plant and another eat. For like the days of a tree will the days of my people be, and my chosen will long enjoy the work of their hands.

**23** They will not labour in vain or bear children for calamity, for they will be the offspring of the blessed of the LORD, and their descendants with them.

**24** Before they call I will answer. While they are yet speaking I will hear.

**25** The wolf and the lamb will graze together. The lion will eat straw like the ox, and dust will be the serpent's food. They will not hurt or destroy in all my holy \[H-QADOSH\] mountain," says the LORD.

### Chapter 66

**1** Thus says the LORD: "Heaven is my throne, and the earth is my footstool. What is the house that you would build for me, and what is the place of my rest?

**2** All these things my hand has made, and so all these things came to be, declares the LORD. But this is the one to whom I will look: the one who is humble and contrite in spirit \[H-RUACH\] and trembles at my word.

**3** The one who slaughters an ox is like one who strikes down a person. The one who sacrifices a lamb is like one who breaks a dog's neck. The one who presents a grain offering is like one who offers pig's blood. The one who makes a memorial offering of frankincense is like one who blesses an idol. These have chosen their own ways, and their soul \[H-NEPHESH\] delights in their abominations.

**4** I also will choose harsh treatment for them and bring their fears on them, because when I called, no one answered. When I spoke, they did not listen, but they did what was evil in my eyes and chose that in which I did not delight."

**5** Hear the word of the LORD, you who tremble at his word: "Your brothers who hate you and cast you out for my name's sake have said, 'Let the LORD be glorified, that we may see your joy.' But it is they who will be put to shame.

**6** The sound of an uproar from the city. A sound from the temple. The sound of the LORD, rendering recompense to his enemies.

**7** Before she was in labour she gave birth. Before her pain came on her she delivered a son.

**8** Who has heard such a thing? Who has seen such things? Shall a land be born in one day? Shall a nation be brought forth in one moment? For as soon as Zion was in labour she brought forth her children.

**9** Shall I bring to the point of birth and not cause to bring forth?" says the LORD. "Shall I who cause to bring forth shut the womb?" says your God.

**10** "Rejoice with Jerusalem, and be glad for her, all you who love her. Rejoice with her in joy, all you who mourn over her,

**11** that you may nurse and be satisfied from her consoling \[H-NACHAM\] breast, that you may drink deeply with delight from her glorious abundance."

**12** For thus says the LORD: "See, I will extend peace \[H-SHALOM\] to her like a river, and the glory of the nations like an overflowing stream. And you will nurse. You will be carried on her hip and bounced on her knees.

**13** As one whom his mother comforts \[H-NACHAM\], so I will comfort you. You will be comforted in Jerusalem.

**14** You will see, and your heart will rejoice. Your bones will flourish like the grass. And the hand of the LORD will be known to his servants, and he will show his indignation against his enemies.

**15** For see, the LORD will come in fire, and his chariots like the whirlwind, to render his anger in fury and his rebuke with flames of fire.

**16** For by fire will the LORD enter into judgement, and by his sword, with all flesh. And those slain by the LORD will be many.

**17** Those who sanctify and purify themselves to go into the gardens, following one in the midst, eating pig's flesh and the abomination and mice, will come to an end together," declares the LORD.

**18** "For I know their works and their thoughts, and the time is coming to gather all nations and tongues. And they will come and will see my glory,

**19** and I will set a sign among them. And from them I will send survivors to the nations, to Tarshish, Put and Lud, who draw the bow, to Tubal and Javan, to the coastlands far away, that have not heard my fame or seen my glory. And they will declare my glory among the nations.

**20** And they will bring all your brothers from all the nations as an offering to the LORD, on horses and in chariots and in litters and on mules and on dromedaries, to my holy \[H-QADOSH\] mountain Jerusalem," says the LORD, "just as the Israelites bring their grain offering in a clean vessel to the house of the LORD.

**21** And some of them also I will take for priests and for Levites," says the LORD.

**22** "For as the new heavens and the new earth that I make will remain before me," declares the LORD, "so your offspring and your name will remain.

**23** From new moon to new moon, and from Sabbath to Sabbath, all flesh will come to worship before me," declares the LORD.

**24** "And they will go out and look on the dead bodies of the people who have rebelled against me. For their worm will not die, their fire will not be quenched, and they will be an abhorrence to all flesh."

*Review note: Isaiah 56 to 66 were drafted in WABV wording. They are the third part of the book ("the new Jerusalem"), with the welcome of the foreigner and the eunuch (56:1-8), the faithless watchmen (56:9-12), the death of the righteous and the false worship (57:1-13), the high and holy One who lives with the contrite (57:14-21), true fasting and the Sabbath (58:1-14), the sins that separate and the arm that saves (59:1-21), the glory that rises on Zion (60:1-22), the anointed one with good news for the poor (61:1-11), the new name of Zion (62:1-12), the one who comes from Edom and the prayer of the people (63:1 to 64:12) and the new heavens and new earth, the humble and contrite, and the final judgement and gathering (65:1 to 66:24). In 56:1, 57:12, 58:8, 59:9, 59:16, 60:17, 60:21, 61:3, 61:10-11, 62:1, 63:1 and 64:5 "righteousness" is tagged H-TSEDAQAH. In 56:4, 59:21, 61:8 and 57:8 "covenant" is tagged H-BERIT. In 56:7, 57:13, 57:15, 58:13, 60:9, 60:14, 62:12, 63:10-11, 63:15, 63:18, 64:10-11, 65:5, 65:11, 65:25 and 66:20 "holy" is tagged H-QADOSH. In 57:15, 57:16, 59:19, 59:21, 61:1, 61:3, 63:10-11, 63:14, 65:14 and 66:2 the word *ruach* is tagged H-RUACH, with "spirit", "Spirit" or "wind" as the sense requires. In 63:10 and 63:11 "his holy Spirit" is *ruach qodsho*, which is used in the Hebrew Bible here and in Psalm 51:11 only, and the footnote records that the word is not read as a name of a person of the Godhead and does not decide the later use. In 57:2, 57:19, 57:21, 59:8, 60:17, 54:10, 66:12 "peace" is tagged H-SHALOM. In 57:9 "Sheol" is tagged H-SHEOL. In 57:18, 61:2 and 66:11-13 "comfort" is tagged H-NACHAM. In 59:20, 62:12, 63:4, 63:9 and 63:16 "Redeemer", "redeemed" and "redemption" are tagged H-GOEL. In 59:20 and 63:17 "turn" and "return" are tagged H-SHUV. In 61:1 "anointed" is tagged H-MASHIACH. In 58:10-11, 61:10 and 66:3 "soul" and "desire" are tagged H-NEPHESH. In 63:7 "steadfast love" is tagged H-HESED. In 58:6-9 the passage on fasting, in 61:1-2 the words that the Gospel of Luke 4:18-19 reads in the synagogue (in the Greek form), and in 65:17 and 66:22 the new heavens and new earth (compare 2 Peter 3:13 and Revelation 21:1) are recorded in the footnotes without comment. In 61:2 "the day of vengeance of our God" is rendered as written, and the footnote records that the verse stops at this point in Luke 4:19. In 63:1-6 the figure from Edom and the winepress is rendered as written, with no softening and no comment. In 65:20 and 66:24 the verses on the death of the young and the worm that does not die are rendered as written, and the footnote records that 66:24 is echoed in Mark 9:48. In 66:17 "following one in the midst" is hard (the reading tradition has "one" in the feminine and the written text in the masculine). In 66:19 the WABV text gives "Put", following the Greek, where the Hebrew text has "Pul", and it departs from the Hebrew. *At 61:8 the WABV text gives "robbery and wrong", following the Dead Sea scroll and the versions (*be-avlah*), where the Hebrew text has "robbery with a burnt offering" (*be-olah*), and it departs from the Hebrew. These are the two departures in the section, and they are flagged for the scholar. The Hebrew is very hard in some places, including 56:10*-12, 57:5-12, 58:3, 59:5, 59:10, 59:19, 60:4, 61:1, 62:5, 63:1, 63:3, 63:9, 63:11, 63:15, 63:19, 64:4-5, 64:7, 65:4-7, 65:11, 66:5, 66:9, 66:17 and 66:19-20. The footnotes record the main differences and the uncertain terms. The section needs a check against the Hebrew by a scholar and an originality check before it is locked.*

## Appendix A: Translation Footnotes

- **1:1** "The vision of Isaiah son of Amoz" translates *chazon Yesha'yahu ben-Amots*. The name *Yesha'yahu* means "the LORD saves" or "salvation of Yah". The kings are Uzziah, Jotham, Ahaz and Hezekiah, in the later eighth century before Christ. The word *chazon* is used for the whole book, and may be read "vision" or "revelation".
- **1:2** "Hear, heavens, and give ear, earth" calls the heavens and the earth as witnesses, as in Deuteronomy 32:1. "Children I have reared" translates *banim gidaleti*.
- **1:3** "Crib" translates *evus*, as in Proverbs 14:4.
- **1:4** "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*, a title that is used about twenty-five times in the book. See entry H-QADOSH. "Offspring of evildoers" translates *zera merei'im*. "They are utterly estranged" translates *nazoru achor*.
- **1:5** "Why will you still be struck down?" translates *al meh tukku od*, which may also be read "where will you be struck again?".
- **1:6** "Pressed out" translates *zoru*.
- **1:7** "As overthrown by foreigners" translates *ke-mahpekhat zarim*, in which *mahpekhah* is the word for the overthrow of Sodom (see Genesis 19:25), and some read *Sedom* ("Sodom") for *zarim* ("foreigners"). The line is hard.
- **1:8** "The daughter of Zion" translates *bat-Tsiyyon*, a figure of the city. "A booth" translates *sukkah*, and "a cucumber field" translates *miqshah*.
- **1:9** "A few survivors" translates *sarid ki-me'at*.
- **1:10** "Rulers" translates *qetsinim*. "Teaching" translates *torat Eloheinu*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **1:11** "Sacrifices" translates *zevachim*, and "burnt offerings" translates *olot*. The words of verses 11 to 15 are rendered as written.
- **1:12** "Trampling" translates *remos*.
- **1:13** "Vain offerings" translates *minchat-shav*, and "incense" translates *qetoret*. "I cannot endure iniquity along with solemn assembly" translates *lo-uchal aven va-atsarah*, which is hard.
- **1:14** "My soul hates" translates *sane'ah nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **1:15** "Blood" translates *damim*, "bloodshed".
- **1:17** "Correct oppression" translates *ashru chamots*, in which *chamots* may mean "the oppressor" or "the oppressed".
- **1:18** "Let us reason together" translates *nivvachechah*, a verb of the court, "let us argue the case". "Scarlet" translates *shanim*, and "crimson" translates *tola*.
- **1:21** "The faithful city" translates *qiryah ne'emanah*, and "prostitute" translates *zonah*. "Righteousness" translates *tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **1:22** "Dross" translates *sigim*, as in Proverbs 25:4.
- **1:23** "Rebels" translates *sorerim*, and "a bribe" translates *shochad*.
- **1:24** "The Mighty One of Israel" translates *avir Yisra'el*, as in Genesis 49:24. "I will get relief" translates *enachem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **1:25** "With lye" translates *ke-bor*, and some read *ke-kur*, "in a furnace".
- **1:26** "The city of righteousness" translates *ir ha-tsedeq*.
- **1:27** "Will be redeemed" translates *tippadeh*. "Those in her who return" translates *shaveha*. See entry H-SHUV. "By righteousness" translates *bi-tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **1:29** "The oaks" translates *eilim*, which refers to the places of idol worship.
- **1:31** "The strong" translates *ha-chason*, a rare word, and "tinder" translates *ne'oret*.
- **2:1** The verse is a second heading, as in 1:1.
- **2:2** "In the latter days" translates *be-acharit ha-yamim*. Verses 2 to 4 are nearly the same as Micah 4:1-3, and the lines that are the same in the Hebrew are given the same wording in the WABV text of Micah 4:1-3, where the footnote records the differences.
- **2:3** "Teaching" translates *torah*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **2:4** "Ploughshares" translates *ittim*, and "pruning hooks" translates *mazmerot*. The saying is reversed in Joel 3:10.
- **2:6** "Full of things from the east" translates *mal'u miqqedem*. "Fortune-tellers" translates *onenim*. "They strike hands" translates *yaspiqu*, which is a rare verb that may mean "strike hands", "clap" or "abound".
- **2:9** "You must not forgive them" translates *ve-al-tissa lahem*, which is hard and may be read as the prophet's prayer or as a statement.
- **2:10** "The terror of the LORD" translates *pachad YHWH*, and "the splendour of his majesty" translates *hadar ge'ono*.
- **2:16** "The ships of Tarshish" translates *oniyyot Tarshish*. "Beautiful craft" translates *sekhiyyot ha-chemdah*, which is hard.
- **2:18** "Utterly pass away" translates *kalil yachalof*, and "idols" translates *elilim*, "nothings".
- **2:20** "Moles" translates *chafor perot*, which is hard and may be read as one word.
- **2:22** "Stop regarding" translates *chidlu lakhem min-ha-adam*. "Breath" translates *neshamah*, a different word from *ruach*. The verse is not in the Greek.
- **3:1** "Support and supply" translates *mash'en u-mash'enah*.
- **3:2** "The diviner" translates *qosem*.
- **3:3** "The skilful magician" translates *chakham charashim*, and "the expert in charms" translates *nevon lachash*.
- **3:4** "Boys" translates *ne'arim*, and "infants" translates *ta'alulim*.
- **3:5** "Will be insolent" translates *yirhavu*, a rare verb.
- **3:6** "A cloak" translates *simlah*.
- **3:7** "A healer" translates *chovesh*, "one who binds up".
- **3:8** "To defy his glorious presence" translates *la-mrot einei khevodo*.
- **3:9** "The look on their faces" translates *hakkarat penehem*. "Woe to their soul" translates *oi le-nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **3:12** "Women rule over them" follows the Hebrew (*nashim*). Some read *noshim*, "creditors", and the Greek has "exactors". "Infants are their oppressors" translates *ne'arim me'olelo*, in which the Hebrew word is hard and may be read "children are their oppressors".
- **3:13** "Peoples" follows the Hebrew (*ammim*). The Greek has "his people".
- **3:14** "The spoil of the poor" translates *gezelat he-ani*.
- **3:17** "Their secret parts" translates *potan*, a rare word that may mean "their foreheads" or "their scalps".
- **3:18** The verses 18 to 23 are a list of ornaments, and the words are mostly rare and uncertain.
- **3:24** "Branding" translates *ki*, a rare word that may mean "branding" or "burning".
- **4:1** "Reproach" translates *cherpah*, the shame of being unmarried.
- **4:2** "The branch of the LORD" translates *tsemach YHWH*, in which *tsemach* means "growth" or "sprout", and is used in other books as a title (see Jeremiah 23:5 and Zechariah 3:8). "Pride and honour" translates *ga'on ve-tiferet*, and "the survivors" translates *pelitat*.
- **4:3** "Called holy" translates *qadosh yei'amer lo*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Recorded for life" translates *kattuv la-chayyim*, as in Exodus 32:32.
- **4:4** "The filth" translates *tso'at*, and "the bloodstains" translates *demei*. "A spirit of judgement and a spirit of burning" translates *be-ruach mishpat u-ve-ruach ba'er*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **4:5** "Create" translates *bara*. "A canopy" translates *chuppah*, which may also mean a wedding canopy.
- **5:1** "My beloved" translates *yedidi*, and "my love song" translates *shirat dodi*. "A very fertile hill" translates *be-qeren ben-shamen*, "on a horn, a son of oil".
- **5:2** "Cleared it of stones" translates *va-yesaqqelehu*. "Choice vines" translates *soreq*, and "wild grapes" translates *be'ushim*, "stinking things".
- **5:7** "Justice ... bloodshed" translates *mishpat ... mispach*, and "righteousness ... an outcry" translates *tsedaqah ... tse'aqah*, in two plays on words. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **5:8** "Join house to house" is as in Micah 2:2.
- **5:9** "In my hearing" translates *be-oznai*.
- **5:10** "Ten acres" translates *tsimdei-kerem*, "ten yokes of vineyard", and "a bath" and "an ephah" are measures of liquid and dry goods.
- **5:11** "Strong drink" translates *shekhar*.
- **5:12** "Lyre" translates *kinnor*, "harp" translates *nevel*, "tambourine" translates *tof*, and "flute" translates *chalil*.
- **5:13** "Go into exile" translates *galah*, in which the verb is the "prophetic past" (the exile is told as if it had happened).
- **5:14** "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL. "Its appetite" translates *nafshah*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **5:16** "The Holy God" translates *ha-El ha-qadosh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Shows himself holy" translates *niqdash*.
- **5:17** "Nomads" translates *gerim*, which may also be read "strangers", and "the ruins of the rich" translates *charavot mechim*, a rare word.
- **5:18** "Cords of falsehood" translates *chavlei ha-shav*, and "cart ropes" translates *avot ha-agalah*.
- **5:19** "The plan of the Holy One of Israel" translates *atsat qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **5:24** "The teaching" translates *torat*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **5:25** "His hand is stretched out still" is a refrain that is repeated in 9:12, 9:17, 9:21 and 10:4. "Their corpses" translates *nivlatam*, and "refuse" translates *sucha*.
- **5:26** "A signal" translates *nes*, and "whistle" translates *sharaq*.
- **5:28** "Seem like flint" translates *ke-tsar nechshevu*, which is hard.
- **6:1** "In the year that King Uzziah died" is about 740 before Christ. "The Lord" translates *Adonai*, and "the train of his robe" translates *shulav*. "The temple" translates *ha-heikhal*.
- **6:2** "The seraphim" translates *serafim*, "burning ones". "His feet" may be a gentle word for another part of the body.
- **6:3** "Holy, holy, holy" translates *qadosh qadosh qadosh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The whole earth is full of his glory" translates *melo khol-ha-arets kevodo*.
- **6:4** "The foundations of the thresholds" translates *amot ha-sippim*.
- **6:5** "I am lost" translates *nidmeti*, which may come from a root that means "be ruined" or from one that means "be silenced". "Unclean lips" translates *temei sefatayim*.
- **6:6** "A burning coal" translates *ritspah*, and "tongs" translates *melqachayim*.
- **6:7** "Atoned for" translates *techupar*. See entry H-KAPPER.
- **6:8** "Who will go for us?" translates *mi yelekh-lanu*, in which "us" refers to the heavenly court.
- **6:9** "Keep on hearing, but do not understand" translates *shim'u shamo'a ve-al-tavinu*. The words of verses 9 and 10 are rendered as written, with no softening. They are quoted in Matthew 13:14-15, Mark 4:12, Luke 8:10, John 12:40 and Acts 28:26-27.
- **6:10** "Turn" translates *shav*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **6:11** "Waste" translates *sho'ah*.
- **6:13** "A tenth" translates *asiriyyah*, "terebinth" translates *elah*, and "oak" translates *allon*. "The holy seed is its stump" translates *zera qodesh matstsevetah*. See entry H-QADOSH. The line is hard, and the Greek lacks it.
- **7:1** The war of Rezin and Pekah against Ahaz is told in 2 Kings 16:5-9, and is dated about 735 before Christ.
- **7:2** "Is in league with Ephraim" translates *nachah Aram al-Efrayim*, "Syria rests on Ephraim", which is hard. "Ephraim" is the northern kingdom.
- **7:3** "Shear-jashub" translates *She'ar yashuv*, "a remnant will return". See entry H-SHUV. "The conduit of the upper pool" and "the Washer's Field" are places near Jerusalem (see 36:2).
- **7:4** "Smouldering stumps" translates *zanvot*, "tails".
- **7:6** "The son of Tabeel" translates *ben-Tavel*.
- **7:7** "The Lord GOD" translates *Adonai YHWH*.
- **7:8** "Within sixty-five years Ephraim will be shattered" is a parenthesis, and the Greek lacks it.
- **7:9** "If you are not firm in faith, you will not be firm at all" translates *im lo ta'aminu ki lo te'amenu*, which has a play on words on the verb *aman*.
- **7:11** "Deep as Sheol" translates *he'meq she'olah*, in which the Hebrew may be read *she'alah*, "make deep your request", or *she'olah*, "to Sheol". See entry H-SHEOL.
- **7:12** "Put the LORD to the test" translates *lo anasseh*.
- **7:13** "House of David" translates *beit David*, and "weary" translates *hal'ot*.
- **7:14** "The young woman" translates *ha-almah*, a word that means a young woman of marriageable age, and that is used in Genesis 24:43, Exodus 2:8, Psalm 68:25, Proverbs 30:19 and the Song of Songs 1:3 and 6:8. The Hebrew word for a virgin in the strict sense is *betulah*. The Greek translates *almah* as *parthenos* ("virgin"), and Matthew 1:23 quotes the Greek. The word has the definite article. "Will conceive" translates *harah*, which may also be read "is pregnant". "Immanuel" translates *Immanu El*, "God with us". The footnote does not decide between the readings of the sign.
- **7:15** "Curds and honey" translates *chem'ah u-devash*.
- **7:17** "The king of Assyria" may be a later addition.
- **7:18** "Whistle" translates *yishrok*. "The streams of Egypt" translates *ye'orei Mitsrayim*.
- **7:20** "A razor that is hired" translates *be-ta'ar ha-sekhirah*. "Beyond the River" is the Euphrates. "The hair of the feet" is a figure for another part of the body.
- **7:23** "A thousand shekels of silver" is as in the Hebrew.
- **8:1** "A large tablet" translates *gillayon gadol*. "In ordinary script" translates *be-cheret enosh*, "in the stylus of a man". "Maher-shalal-hash-baz" translates *Maher shalal chash baz*, "quick to the plunder, swift to the spoil".
- **8:2** "Reliable witnesses" translates *edim ne'emanim*. Uriah the priest is as in 2 Kings 16:10-16.
- **8:3** "The prophetess" translates *ha-nevi'ah*, a word that may refer to the wife of Isaiah.
- **8:6** "The waters of Shiloah that flow gently" translates *mei ha-Shiloach ha-holekhim le-at*. "Rejoices over" translates *mesos*, which is hard and may be read "melts with fear of" (as some read it).
- **8:7** "The River" is the Euphrates.
- **8:8** "Immanuel" is the address to the land, as in 7:14.
- **8:9** "Be broken" translates *ro'u*, a verb that may be read "be broken" or "make an uproar".
- **8:10** "For God is with us" translates *ki immanu El*.
- **8:11** "His strong hand" translates *chezqat ha-yad*.
- **8:12** "Conspiracy" translates *qesher*.
- **8:13** "Regard as holy" translates *taqdishu*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **8:14** "A sanctuary" translates *le-miqdash*. See entry H-QADOSH. Some read *le-moqesh*, "a snare". "A stone of offence and a rock of stumbling" is quoted in Romans 9:33 and 1 Peter 2:8.
