- **8:16** "The testimony" translates *te'udah*, "the teaching" translates *torah* (see entry H-TORAH), and "my disciples" translates *limmudai*.
- **8:18** "Signs and portents" translates *otot u-moftim*.
- **8:19** "The mediums" translates *ha-ovot*, and "the necromancers" translates *ha-yidde'onim*. "Who chirp and mutter" translates *ha-matsaftsefim ve-ha-maghim*. The practice is forbidden in Leviticus 19:31 and Deuteronomy 18:11.
- **8:20** "To the teaching and to the testimony" translates *le-torah ve-li-te'udah*. See entry H-TORAH. "They have no dawn" translates *asher ein lo shachar*.
- **8:21** "Turn their faces upward" is hard.
- **8:22** "Thick darkness" translates *afelah*.
- **9:1** In the Hebrew this verse is 8:23, and English 9:1-21 is Hebrew 8:23 to 9:20. "But there will be no gloom" translates *ki lo mu'af*. "The way of the sea" translates *derekh ha-yam*, and "Galilee of the nations" translates *Gelil ha-goyim*. The verse is quoted in Matthew 4:15-16.
- **9:2** "Deep darkness" translates *tsalmavet*, as in Psalm 23:4.
- **9:3** "You have increased its joy" follows the reading tradition (*lo*, "to it"). The written text has *lo*, "not", so that it reads "you have not increased the joy".
- **9:4** "The day of Midian" refers to Judges 7.
- **9:5** "Boot of the tramping warrior" translates *se'on so'en*, a rare pair of words.
- **9:6** "To us a child is born, to us a son is given" translates *ki-yeled yullad lanu ben nittan lanu*. "The government" translates *ha-misrah*, a rare word. The four names are *pele yo'ets* ("Wonderful Counsellor", which may also be read "Wonderful, Counsellor" or "a wonder of a counsellor"), *El gibbor* ("Mighty God", which may also be read "divine hero"), *Avi-ad* ("Everlasting Father", literally "father of eternity") and *sar-shalom* ("Prince of Peace"). See entry H-SHALOM. The Greek has "Angel of Great Counsel".
- **9:7** "Justice" translates *mishpat*, and "righteousness" translates *tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "The zeal of the LORD of hosts" translates *qin'at YHWH tseva'ot*.
- **9:10** "Dressed stones" translates *gazit*, and "sycamores" translates *shiqmim*.
- **9:11** "The adversaries of Rezin" translates *tsarei Retsin*, which is hard.
- **9:12** "His hand is stretched out still" is a refrain, as in 5:25, 9:17, 9:21 and 10:4.
- **9:14** "Palm branch and reed" translates *kippah ve-agmon*.
- **9:15** "The elder and honoured person" translates *zaqen u-nesu panim*.
- **9:17** "Godless" translates *chanef*, and "folly" translates *nevalah*.
- **9:19** "Is scorched" translates *ne'tam*, a rare verb.
- **9:20** "The flesh of his own arm" translates *besar zero'o*. The Greek has "the flesh of his neighbour".
- **10:1** "Decree iniquitous decrees" translates *chokekim chiqqei-aven*.
- **10:3** "The day of punishment" translates *yom pequddah*.
- **10:4** "Nothing remains but to crouch among the prisoners" translates *bilti kara tachat assir*, in which *bilti* means "without" or "except", and the line is hard and may be read "without me they crouch among the prisoners".
- **10:5** "The rod of my anger" translates *shevet appi*.
- **10:9** The cities are Calno, Carchemish, Hamath, Arpad, Samaria and Damascus, which the Assyrians took.
- **10:13** "Boundaries" translates *gevulot*. "Like a bull" translates *ke-abbir*, and "those who sit on thrones" translates *yoshevim*, which may also be read "inhabitants".
- **10:16** "Wasting sickness" translates *razon*.
- **10:17** "His Holy One" translates *qedosho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **10:18** "Both soul and body" translates *me-nefesh ve-ad-basar*, "from soul to flesh". See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **10:19** "A child can write them down" translates *na'ar yichtevem*.
- **10:20** "Will lean" translates *nish'an*. "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **10:21** "A remnant will return" translates *she'ar yashuv*, as in the name Shear-jashub in 7:3. See entry H-SHUV. "The mighty God" translates *El gibbor*, as in 9:6.
- **10:22** "Destruction is decreed, overflowing with righteousness" translates *kilayon charuts shotef tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The verses 22 and 23 are quoted in Romans 9:27-28.
- **10:23** "A full end, as decreed" translates *kalah ve-nechratsah*.
- **10:26** "The rock of Oreb" is as in Judges 7:25.
- **10:27** "Because of fatness" translates *mi-penei shamen*, which is hard and may be read "because of the anointed one" or "from before the oil".
- **10:28** The verses 28 to 32 are a list of places on the road from the north to Jerusalem.
- **10:33** "The boughs" translates *pe'orah*, a rare word.
- **10:34** "Majestic trees" translates *addir*.
- **11:1** "A shoot" translates *choter*, and "a branch" translates *netser*. "The stump" translates *geza*.
- **11:2** "The Spirit of the LORD" translates *ruach YHWH*. See entry H-RUACH. The six are wisdom (*chokhmah*), understanding (*binah*), counsel (*etsah*), might (*gevurah*), knowledge (*da'at*) and the fear of the LORD (*yirat YHWH*). The Greek has seven, with "piety" added.
- **11:3** "His delight" translates *va-hariho*, a rare verb that may mean "his scent" or "his breathing in".
- **11:4** "With righteousness" translates *be-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "The poor" translates *dallim*, and "the meek" translates *anavim*. "The rod of his mouth" translates *shevet pivv*, in which some read *erets* ("the earth") as *aritsim* ("the ruthless"). "The breath of his lips" translates *ruach sefatav*. See entry H-RUACH. The line is echoed in 2 Thessalonians 2:8.
- **11:5** "The belt" translates *ezor*, and "faithfulness" translates *emunah*.
- **11:6** "A fattened calf" translates *meri*. "A little child will lead them" translates *ve-na'ar qatan nohe'ag bam*.
- **11:8** "The cobra" translates *peten*, and "the adder" translates *tsif'oni*.
- **11:9** "My holy mountain" translates *har qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The earth will be full of the knowledge of the LORD" is as in Habakkuk 2:14.
- **11:10** "The root of Jesse" translates *shoresh Yishai*, and "a signal" translates *nes*. The verse is quoted in Romans 15:12 in the Greek form.
- **11:11** "A second time" translates *shenit*, and the places are Pathros (upper Egypt), Cush, Elam, Shinar, Hamath and the coastlands.
- **11:15** "Utterly destroy" translates *veheherim*, which is the verb of the ban. "His scorching wind" translates *ba-ayyam ruchO*, in which *ayyam* is a rare word. See entry H-RUACH.
- **11:16** "A highway" translates *mesillah*.
- **12:1** "Comfort" translates *ve-tenachameni*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **12:2** "God is my salvation" translates *El yeshu'ati*. "Yah, the LORD, is my strength and my song" translates *ozzi ve-zimrat Yah YHWH*, as in Exodus 15:2 and Psalm 118:14, and the wording is matched. "Salvation" translates *yeshu'ah*.
- **12:3** "The wells of salvation" translates *ma'yenei ha-yeshu'ah*.
- **12:4** "Is exalted" translates *nisgav*.
- **12:6** "Inhabitant of Zion" translates *yoshevet Tsiyyon*. "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **13:1** "The oracle" translates *massa*, which may also be read "burden". It is the heading of each oracle in 13 to 23 and is used in Proverbs 30:1 and 31:1. "Saw" translates *chazah*.
- **13:2** "A bare hill" translates *har nishpeh*, and "the gates of the nobles" translates *pitchei nedivim*.
- **13:3** "My consecrated ones" translates *mequddashai*. See entry H-QADOSH. They may be the Medes of verse 17 or heavenly warriors.
- **13:6** "The Almighty" translates *Shaddai*, and "destruction" translates *shod*, in a play on words.
- **13:8** "Their faces will be aflame" translates *penei lehavim penehem*.
- **13:10** "Their constellations" translates *kesileihem*, "their Orions".
- **13:12** "The gold of Ophir" refers to a land that is not known (see 1 Kings 9:28).
- **13:16** "Their infants will be dashed in pieces" is rendered as written, with no softening and no comment. See also Psalm 137:9, Hosea 13:16 and Nahum 3:10.
- **13:17** "The Medes" translates *Madai*. The Medes and Persians took Babylon in 539 before Christ.
- **13:19** "Like Sodom and Gomorrah" refers to Genesis 19.
- **13:21** "Howling creatures" translates *ochim*, a rare word. "Ostriches" translates *benot ya'anah*, and "wild goats" translates *se'irim*.
- **13:22** "Hyenas" translates *iyyim*, and "jackals" translates *tannim*.
- **14:1** "Will have compassion" translates *yerachem*, from *racham*.
- **14:2** "As male and female slaves" translates *la-avadim ve-shifchot*.
- **14:4** "The taunt" translates *mashal*. "The oppressor" translates *nogesh*, and "the insolent fury" translates *madhevah*, a rare word that is read *marhevah* in the Dead Sea scroll.
- **14:8** "The cypresses" translates *berosh*.
- **14:9** "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL. "The shades" translates *refa'im*. "Leaders" translates *attudim*, literally "he-goats".
- **14:11** "Pomp" translates *ge'on*, "harps" translates *nevel*, and "maggots" translates *rimmah*.
- **14:12** "Day Star" translates *helel*, from a root that means "shine", and "son of Dawn" translates *ben shachar*. The Latin Bible gives *Lucifer*, "light-bearer", for the word. The WABV text does not use that name. The figure is the king of Babylon in this taunt.
- **14:13** "The stars of God" translates *kokhevei-El*, and "the mount of assembly in the far reaches of the north" translates *har mo'ed be-yarketei tsafon*.
- **14:14** "The Most High" translates *Elyon*.
- **14:15** "The pit" translates *bor*.
- **14:19** "A loathed branch" translates *netser nit'av*, which is hard. The Greek has "a dead body that is loathed".
- **14:20** "The offspring of evildoers" translates *zera mere'im*.
- **14:23** "The hedgehog" translates *qippod*, and "the broom of destruction" translates *be-matate ha-shemed*, a rare phrase.
- **14:24** The verses 24 to 27 give the plan of the LORD against Assyria, and the hand that is stretched out (see 9:12).
- **14:28** "The year that King Ahaz died" is about 715 before Christ.
- **14:29** "The rod" translates *shevet*, "an adder" translates *tsif'oni*, and "a flying fiery serpent" translates *saraf me'ofef*.
- **14:30** "The firstborn of the poor" translates *bekhorei dallim*.
- **14:31** "No straggler in his ranks" translates *ein boded be-mo'adav*, which is hard.
- **14:32** "The messengers of the nation" translates *malakhei-goy*, and "has founded Zion" translates *yissad Tsiyyon*.
- **15:1** The verses 1 to 9 are a list of the places of Moab. "Is undone" translates *nidmah*.
- **15:2** "The temple" translates *ha-bayit*, which may also be a place name (Bayith). "Baldness ... every beard is cut off" are signs of mourning.
- **15:4** "His soul trembles" translates *nafsho yare'ah lo*, which is hard. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **15:5** "Eglath-shelishiyah" may mean "the third Eglath" or "a heifer of three years".
- **15:7** "The Brook of the Willows" translates *nachal ha-aravim*.
- **15:9** "Dibon" follows the Dead Sea scroll and the versions. The Hebrew has *Dimon*, which may be a play on *dam* ("blood"). The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **16:1** "The lambs" translates *kar*, and "Sela" is a place in Edom or Moab.
- **16:2** "The fords of the Arnon" is the boundary of Moab.
- **16:3** "Give counsel, grant justice" translates *havi'u etsah asu felilah*, which is hard, and the words are the Moabites' request to Judah.
- **16:5** "Steadfast love" translates *chesed*. See entry H-HESED. "Faithfulness" translates *emet*. "Righteousness" translates *tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **16:6** "His boasts are false" translates *bad-hu lo-chen*, which is hard.
- **16:7** "The raisin cakes" translates *ashishei*, and "utterly stricken" translates *nekha'im*.
- **16:8** "The vine of Sibmah" is a figure of a wine region of Moab.
- **16:11** "My inner parts" translates *me'ai*, and "lyre" translates *kinnor*.
- **16:12** "The high place" translates *bamah*, and "his sanctuary" translates *miqdasho*.
- **16:14** "Like the years of a hired worker" means a term that is counted exactly.
- **17:1** "A heap of ruins" translates *me'i*.
- **17:2** "The cities of Aroer are deserted" translates *azuvot arei Aro'er*, which is hard. The Greek has "for ever".
- **17:3** "The fortress" translates *mivtsar*.
- **17:5** "The Valley of Rephaim" translates *emeq Refa'im*.
- **17:6** "Gleanings" translates *ulelot*.
- **17:7** "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **17:8** "The Asherim" translates *ha-asherim*, and "the altars of incense" translates *ha-chammanim*.
- **17:9** "The wooded heights" translates *ha-choresh*, which is hard.
- **17:10** "The Rock of your refuge" translates *tsur uzzekh*. "Pleasant plants" translates *nit'ei na'amanim*, which may refer to gardens of Adonis. "The vine-slips of a foreign god" translates *zemorat zar*.
- **17:11** "Grief" translates *nechalah*, and "incurable pain" translates *kheav anush*, which is hard.
- **17:12** The verses 12 to 14 are a short poem on the nations that roar and are rebuked.
- **18:1** "Land of whirring wings" translates *erets tsil tselal kenafayim*, which is hard and may refer to the insects or the sailing ships of Cush, or to the wings of the land. "The rivers of Cush" are the upper Nile and its branches.
- **18:2** "Ambassadors" translates *tsirim*. "Papyrus" translates *gome*. "Tall and smooth-skinned" translates *memushshakh u-morat*, two rare words.
- **18:4** "I will quietly look" translates *eshqotah ve-abbitah*, and "like clear heat in sunshine" translates *kechom tsach alei or*.
- **18:5** "Pruning knives" translates *zalzallim*, a rare word.
- **18:7** "Gifts" translates *shai*, as in Psalm 68:31.
- **19:1** "A swift cloud" translates *av qal*.
- **19:3** "The spirit" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "Emptied out" translates *nevqah*, and "the sorcerers" translates *ittim*.
- **19:5** "The sea" is the Nile.
- **19:6** "Canals" translates *naharot*, which may also mean "branches".
- **19:9** "Combed flax" translates *pishtim serequot*, and "white cotton" translates *chorai*.
- **19:10** "The pillars of the land" translates *shatotheha*, and "all who work for pay" translates *kol-osei sekhar*, which is hard.
- **19:11** "Zoan" translates *Tso'an*, an Egyptian city. "Stupid" translates *nivar*.
- **19:13** "Memphis" translates *Nof*, and "cornerstones" translates *pinnat*.
- **19:14** "A spirit of confusion" translates *ruach iv'im*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **19:15** "Head or tail, palm branch or reed" is as in 9:14.
- **19:17** "A terror" translates *chagga*, a rare word.
- **19:18** "The language of Canaan" translates *sefat Kena'an*, that is Hebrew. "The City of Destruction" translates *ir ha-heres*, which follows the Hebrew text most often read. Some manuscripts and the Dead Sea scroll have *ir ha-cheres*, "the City of the Sun" (Heliopolis).
- **19:19** "A pillar" translates *matstsevah*.
- **19:20** "A saviour and defender" translates *moshia ve-rav*.
- **19:22** "Striking and healing" translates *nagof ve-rafo*. "They will return" translates *ve-shavu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **19:25** "My people" translates *ammi*, and "the work of my hands" translates *ma'aseh yadai*.
- **20:1** "The commander in chief" translates *ha-tartan*. The capture of Ashdod by Sargon was in 711 before Christ.
- **20:2** "The sackcloth" translates *ha-saq*.
- **20:3** "Naked" translates *arom*, which may mean stripped to the undergarment. "For three years" may belong to "walked" or to "a sign".
- **20:4** "With buttocks uncovered" translates *chasufei shet*, a rare phrase.
- **21:1** "The wilderness of the sea" translates *midbar-yam*, a name for Babylon. "A terrible land" translates *erets nora'ah*.
- **21:2** "A stern vision" translates *chazut kashah*. "The traitor betrays" translates *ha-boged boged*.
- **21:3** "My loins" translates *motnai*.
- **21:4** "The twilight I longed for" translates *neshef chishqi*.
- **21:5** "They spread the rugs" translates *tsafoh ha-tsafit*, a rare phrase. "Oil the shield" refers to preparing it for battle.
- **21:6** "A watchman" translates *ha-metsappeh*.
- **21:8** "The one who saw cried out" follows the Dead Sea scroll (*ha-ro'eh*). The Hebrew text has *aryeh*, "a lion", so that it reads "a lion cried out". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **21:9** "Fallen, fallen is Babylon" translates *naflah naflah Bavel*. It is as in Revelation 14:8 and 18:2 in the Greek.
- **21:10** "My threshed and winnowed one" translates *mudashati u-ven-gorni*, "my threshing and the son of my threshing floor".
- **21:11** "Dumah" means "silence" and is a name for Edom or a place in Arabia. "Seir" is Edom. "Watchman, what of the night?" translates *shomer mah-millayil*.
- **21:12** The answer of the watchman is hard.
- **21:13** "In Arabia" translates *ba-arav*, which may also be read "in the evening". "Dedanites" translates *Dedanim*.
- **21:16** "According to the years of a hired worker" is as in 16:14.
- **22:1** "The valley of vision" translates *gei chizzayon*, a name for Jerusalem.
- **22:3** "Without the bow they were captured" translates *mi-beli qeshet ussaru*, which is hard.
- **22:5** "Tumult and trampling and confusion" translates *mehumah u-mevusah u-mevukhah*. "A breaking down of walls and a shouting to the mountains" translates *meqarqar qir ve-shoa el-har*, which is hard.
- **22:6** "Elam" and "Kir" are the people and place of the attackers.
- **22:8** "The covering of Judah" translates *masakh Yehudah*, and "the House of the Forest" translates *beit ha-ya'ar* (see 1 Kings 7:2).
- **22:9** The verses 9 to 11 refer to the water works of Hezekiah (see 2 Kings 20:20 and 2 Chronicles 32:30).
- **22:11** "The one who did it" is the LORD.
- **22:13** "Let us eat and drink, for tomorrow we die" is as in 1 Corinthians 15:32 in the Greek.
- **22:14** "Has revealed himself in my ears" translates *niglah be-oznai*. "Will not be atoned for" translates *im-yekhuppar*. See entry H-KAPPER.
- **22:15** "This steward" translates *ha-soken ha-zeh*, and "Shebna" translates *Shevna*. See 36:3.
- **22:16** "Carve a dwelling" translates *chotsevi*.
- **22:17** "Will hurl you away violently" translates *metaltelekha taltelah gaver*, which is hard. "A ball" translates *dur*.
- **22:20** "Eliakim the son of Hilkiah" is as in 36:3.
- **22:21** "Your robe" translates *kuttontekha*, "your sash" translates *avnetekha*, and "your authority" translates *memshaltekha*.
- **22:22** "The key of the house of David" translates *maftei'ach beit David*. It is as in Revelation 3:7 in the Greek.
- **22:23** "A peg" translates *yated*, and "a secure place" translates *maqom ne'eman*. "A throne of honour" translates *kisse kavod*.
- **22:24** "The offspring and issue" translates *ha-tse'etsa'im*, and "flagons" translates *nevalim*.
- **22:25** "The peg ... will give way" may refer to Shebna or to Eliakim, and is hard.
- **23:1** "Ships of Tarshish" translates *oniyyot Tarshish*. "The land of Cyprus" translates *erets Kittim*. "Without house or harbour" translates *mi-bayit mi-bo*, which is hard.
- **23:3** "Shihor" is a name of the Nile, and "revenue" translates *tevu'ah*.
- **23:4** "The sea has spoken" is hard, and the words are said by the sea to Sidon.
- **23:7** "Exultant city" translates *alizah*.
- **23:8** "The bestower of crowns" translates *ha-ma'atirah*.
- **23:10** "Cross over your land like the Nile" translates *ivri artsekh ka-ye'or*. "Restraint" translates *mezach*, a rare word for a belt.
- **23:12** "Oppressed young daughter of Sidon" translates *ha-me'ushshaqah betulat bat-Tsidon*, in which "virgin daughter" is a figure of the city. "Cyprus" translates *Kittim*.
- **23:13** The verse is hard. "This is the people that was not" translates *zeh ha-am lo hayah*, which may refer to Babylon or to Assyria.
- **23:15** "Seventy years, like the days of one king" is as in Jeremiah 25:11-12.
- **23:16** "Take a harp" translates *qechi kinnor*, and "forgotten prostitute" translates *zonah nishkachah*.
- **23:17** "Will visit" translates *yifqod*, and "her wages" translates *etnanah*.
- **23:18** "Holy to the LORD" translates *qodesh la-YHWH*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Fine clothing" translates *mikhseh atiq*, which may also mean "lasting clothing".
- **24:1** "Will empty the earth and make it desolate" translates *boqeq ha-arets u-voleqah*, in a play on words. "Twist its surface" translates *ve-ivvah panehah*.
- **24:4** "The highest people of the earth" translates *merom am ha-arets*.
- **24:5** "Defiled" translates *chanefah*. "The laws" translates *torot*, and may be read "the teachings" (see entry H-TORAH). "The everlasting covenant" translates *berit olam*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **24:6** "A curse" translates *alah*. "Suffer for their guilt" translates *ve-yeshemu*, and "are scorched" translates *charu*. The verse is hard.
- **24:10** "The city of chaos" translates *qiryat tohu*.
- **24:13** "As when an olive tree is beaten" is as in 17:6.
- **24:14** "From the west" translates *miyyam*, "from the sea".
- **24:15** "In the east" translates *ba-urim*, which is hard and may also be read "in the lights", "in the fires" or "in the Urim".
- **24:16** "Songs of praise" translates *zemirot*. "Glory to the Righteous One" translates *tsevi la-tsaddiq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "I pine away" translates *razi li*, a rare word. "The treacherous" translates *bogedim*.
- **24:17** "Terror and the pit and the snare" translates *pachad va-fachat va-fach*, a play on words.
- **24:18** "The windows of heaven" translates *arubbot mi-marom*, as in Genesis 7:11.
- **24:22** "They will be gathered together as prisoners in a pit" translates *ve-ussefu asefah assir al-bor*, which is hard.
- **24:23** "Confounded" translates *chafera*, and "ashamed" translates *bosha*.
- **25:1** "Plans formed of old" translates *atsot merachoq*, and "faithful and sure" translates *emunah omen*.
- **25:2** "The foreigners' palace" translates *armon zarim*.
- **25:4** "The breath of the ruthless" translates *ruach aritsim*, in which *ruach* may mean "breath" or "blast".
- **25:5** "The noise" translates *sha'on*.
- **25:6** "A feast of rich food" translates *mishteh shemanim*. "Well-aged wine" translates *shemarim*, which means wine on its lees. "Full of marrow" translates *memuchayim*.
- **25:7** "The covering ... the veil" translates *ha-lot ... ha-massekhah*, which is hard.
- **25:8** "He will swallow up death for ever" translates *billa ha-mavet la-netsach*. It is quoted in 1 Corinthians 15:54 in the Greek form ("death is swallowed up in victory"). "Will wipe away tears from all faces" is as in Revelation 21:4. "The Lord GOD" translates *Adonai YHWH*. "The reproach" translates *cherpat*.
- **25:9** "We have waited" translates *qivvinu*.
- **25:10** "A dung pit" translates *madmenah*, which has a sound like the name Madmen, a town of Moab.
- **25:11** "The skill of his hands" translates *arvot yadav*, which is hard.
- **26:1** "Walls and bulwarks" translates *chomot va-chel*.
- **26:2** "Keeps faith" translates *shomer emunim*.
- **26:3** "In perfect peace" translates *shalom shalom*, "peace, peace". See entry H-SHALOM. "Whose mind is stayed on you" translates *yetser samuch*, which is hard and may be read "the one whose purpose is firm".
- **26:4** "Yah, the LORD" translates *Yah YHWH*, as in 12:2. "An everlasting rock" translates *tsur olamim*.
- **26:7** "Level" translates *meisharim*, and "Upright One" translates *yashar*. The line is hard.
- **26:8** "Your judgements" translates *mishpatekha*. "The desire of our soul" translates *ta'avat nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **26:9** "My soul" translates *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "My spirit" translates *ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH. "Righteousness" translates *tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **26:10** "If favour is shown" translates *yuchan*.
- **26:11** "Your hand is lifted up" translates *ramah yadekha*, and "the fire for your adversaries" translates *esh tsarekha*. The verse is hard.
- **26:12** "You will ordain peace" translates *tishpot shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **26:13** "Other lords besides you" translates *adonim zulatekha*.
- **26:14** "The shades" translates *refa'im*.
- **26:16** "A whispered prayer" translates *lachash*, a rare word.
- **26:18** "We have given birth to wind" translates *yaladnu ruach*, in which *ruach* is "wind", and the verse is hard.
- **26:19** "Your dead will live" translates *yichyu metekha*. "Their bodies" follows the Dead Sea scroll and the versions. The Hebrew has *nevelati*, "my corpse". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar. "Awake and sing for joy" translates *haqitsu ve-ranenu*. "A dew of light" translates *tal orot*, which is hard. "The earth will give birth to the dead" translates *ve-arets refa'im tappil*, "the earth will let the shades fall". The verse is one of the clearest statements of the rising of the dead in the Hebrew text, and it may also be read as a figure of the restoration of the nation (compare Ezekiel 37 and Daniel 12:2). The footnote does not decide.
- **26:20** "Your chambers" translates *chadarekha*, and "the fury" translates *za'am*.
- **26:21** "Will disclose the blood shed on it" translates *ve-gillah ha-arets et-damehah*.
- **27:1** "Leviathan" translates *livyatan*, "the fleeing serpent" translates *nachash bariach*, "the twisting serpent" translates *nachash aqallaton*, and "the dragon" translates *ha-tannin*. See Job 41 and Psalm 74:14.
- **27:2** "A pleasant vineyard" follows many Hebrew manuscripts and the versions (*kerem chemed*). The Hebrew text most often read has *kerem chemer*, which may mean "a vineyard of wine". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **27:3** "Its keeper" translates *notsrah*, and "I water it" translates *ashqennah*.
- **27:4** "I have no wrath" translates *chemah ein li*. "Thorns and briers" translates *shamir shayit*. The verse is hard.
- **27:5** "My protection" translates *ma'uzzi*.
- **27:6** "Will take root" translates *yashresh*, and "will blossom" translates *yatsits*.
- **27:8** "Measure by measure" translates *be-sa'ah be-sa'ah*, which is hard. "His fierce wind" translates *ruacho ha-qashah*. See entry H-RUACH. "The east wind" translates *qadim*.
- **27:9** "Atoned for" translates *yekhuppar*. See entry H-KAPPER. "Chalkstones" translates *avnei-gir*, and "incense altars" translates *chammanim*.
- **27:10** The verse is hard.
- **27:11** "A people without discernment" translates *lo am binot hu*. "Will not have compassion" translates *lo yerachamennu*, from *racham*.
- **27:12** "The Brook of Egypt" translates *nachal Mitsrayim*. "Will thresh out the grain" translates *yachbot*, and "you will be gleaned" translates *tulqetu*.
- **27:13** "A great trumpet" translates *shofar gadol*. "The holy mountain" translates *har ha-qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH. The verse is echoed in Matthew 24:31.
- **28:1** "The proud crown of the drunkards of Ephraim" translates *atarat ge'ut shikkorei Efrayim*, a figure of Samaria on its hill. "The fading flower" translates *tsits nobel*, and "the rich valley" translates *gei shemanim*, "the valley of fatness".
- **28:2** "A storm of hail, a destroying tempest" translates *zerem barad se'ar qatev*.
- **28:4** "A first-ripe fig" translates *bikkurah*.
- **28:5** "A crown of glory, and a diadem of beauty" translates *atarat tsevi ve-tsefirat tif'eret*.
- **28:6** "A spirit of justice" translates *ruach mishpat*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **28:7** "Reel" translates *shagu*, and "they are swallowed" translates *nivle'u*. "They stumble in giving judgement" translates *pakku pelilijjah*, in which *pelilijjah* is a rare word.
- **28:8** "Filthy vomit" translates *qi tso'ah*.
- **28:10** "Precept upon precept, line upon line" translates *tsav la-tsav tsav la-tsav qav la-qav qav la-qav ze'er sham ze'er sham*, which may be the words of the drunken leaders who mock the prophet (as baby-talk or as a repeated sound), or a rendering of the prophet's own teaching. The footnote does not decide. It is repeated in verse 13.
- **28:11** "By people of strange lips and with a foreign tongue" translates *be-sifat la'ag u-veh-lashon acheret*, a reference to the Assyrians. It is quoted in 1 Corinthians 14:21.
- **28:12** "Rest" translates *menuchah*.
- **28:15** "A covenant" translates *berit*. See entry H-BERIT. "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL. "An agreement" translates *chozeh*. "The overwhelming whip" translates *shot shotef*.
- **28:16** "The Lord GOD" translates *Adonai YHWH*. "A tested stone" translates *even bochan*, "a precious cornerstone" translates *pinnat yiqrat*, and "of a sure foundation" translates *mussad mussad*. "Whoever believes will not be in haste" translates *ha-ma'amin lo yachish*. It is quoted in Romans 9:33 and 10:11 and in 1 Peter 2:6 in the Greek form ("will not be put to shame").
- **28:17** "The line ... the plumb line" translates *qav ... mishqelet*.
- **28:19** "The message" translates *shemu'ah*.
- **28:21** "Mount Perazim" and "the Valley of Gibeon" refer to the victories of David (see 2 Samuel 5:20-25). "Strange" translates *zar*, and "alien" translates *nokhriyyah*.
- **28:22** "Your bonds" translates *moseresekhem*.
- **28:25** "Dill" translates *qetsach*, "cumin" translates *kammon*, "in rows" translates *sorah*, "in its proper place" translates *nisman*, and "emmer" translates *kussemet*. The words are rare and uncertain.
- **28:27** "A threshing sledge" translates *charuts*, and "a cart wheel" translates *ofan agalah*.
- **28:29** "Wonderful in counsel" translates *hifli etsah*, and "excellent in wisdom" translates *higdil tushiyyah*.
- **29:1** "Ariel" may mean "lion of God" or "altar hearth", and is a name for Jerusalem. "Let the feasts run their round" translates *chaggim yinqofu*.
- **29:2** "Moaning and lamentation" translates *ta'aniyyah va-aniyyah*.
- **29:4** "A ghost" translates *ov*.
- **29:5** "Small dust" translates *avaq daq*, and "passing chaff" translates *motz over*.
- **29:6** "You will be visited" translates *tippaqed*.
- **29:7** "Her stronghold" translates *metsodatah*.
- **29:9** "Stupefy yourselves" translates *hitmahmehu*, a rare verb.
- **29:10** "A spirit of deep sleep" translates *ruach tardemah*. See entry H-RUACH. The words "the prophets" and "the seers" may be a later explanation.
- **29:11** "Sealed" translates *chatum*.
- **29:13** "Draw near with their mouth" is quoted in Matthew 15:8-9 and Mark 7:6-7 in the Greek form.
- **29:14** "Wonder upon wonder" translates *hafle va-fele*.
- **29:16** "You turn things upside down" translates *hafkekhem*. The saying of the potter and the clay is echoed in Romans 9:20-21.
- **29:19** "The meek" translates *anavim*, and "will obtain fresh joy" translates *yosifu simchah*. "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **29:20** "The ruthless" translates *aritz*, and "the scoffer" translates *letz*.
- **29:21** "Make a person out to be an offender" translates *machti'ei adam be-davar*, and "with an empty plea" translates *ba-tohu*.
- **29:22** "Who redeemed Abraham" translates *asher padah et-Avraham*, which refers to a story that is not told in Genesis.
- **29:23** "They will sanctify my name" translates *yaqdishu shemi*, and "the Holy One of Jacob" translates *qedosh Ya'aqov*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **29:24** "Those who err in spirit" translates *to'ei ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "Those who murmur" translates *rogenim*.
- **30:1** "Stubborn children" translates *banim sorerim*. "An alliance" translates *massekhah*, from a verb that means "pour out" (a libation), so that it may be read "a treaty that is sealed with a drink offering". "Of my Spirit" translates *ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **30:2** "The protection" translates *ma'oz*, and "the shadow" translates *tsel*.
- **30:4** "Zoan" and "Hanes" are cities of Egypt.
- **30:6** "An oracle on the beasts of the Negeb" translates *massa behemot negev*. "The flying fiery serpent" translates *saraf me'ofef*, as in 14:29.
- **30:7** "Rahab who sits still" translates *Rahav hem shabet*, in which *Rahav* is the name of a sea monster and a name of Egypt (see Psalm 87:4 and 89:10). The Hebrew is hard.
- **30:9** "The instruction" translates *torat YHWH*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **30:10** "Smooth things" translates *chalaqot*, and "illusions" translates *mahatallot*.
- **30:12** "Oppression" translates *osheq*, and "perverseness" translates *nelloz*.
- **30:14** "A shard" translates *cheres*, and "the hearth" translates *yaqud*.
- **30:15** "In returning and rest" translates *be-shuvah va-nachat*. See entry H-SHUV. The word *shuvah* may mean "returning", "repentance" or "sitting still". "Quietness" translates *hashqet*, and "trust" translates *bitchah*.
- **30:16** "Swift" translates *qallu*, in a play on words with *nanus* ("we will flee").
- **30:17** "A flagstaff" translates *toren*, and "a signal" translates *nes*.
- **30:18** "Waits to be gracious" translates *yechakkeh la-chenenkhem*, and "exalts himself" translates *yarum*. "A God of justice" translates *Elohei mishpat*.
- **30:20** "The bread of adversity" translates *lechem tsar*. "Your Teacher" translates *moreikha*, a plural form that is used for one teacher.
- **30:22** "Carved idols" translates *pesilei*, and "metal images" translates *massekhat*. "Unclean things" translates *niddah*, and "Be gone" translates *tse*.
- **30:24** "Seasoned fodder" translates *belil chamits*.
- **30:25** "The day of the great slaughter" translates *yom hereg rav*.
- **30:27** "The name of the LORD" translates *shem YHWH*. "In thick rising smoke" translates *ve-khovedh massa'ah*, which is hard.
- **30:28** "His breath" translates *ruchO*. See entry H-RUACH. "A sieve of destruction" translates *na'fat shav*, and "a bridle" translates *resen*.
- **30:29** "A holy feast" translates *hitqaddesh-chag*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **30:30** "His majestic voice" translates *hod qolo*, and "a cloudburst" translates *nefets*.
- **30:32** The verse is hard.
- **30:33** "A burning place" translates *tofteh*, which has a sound like Topheth, the place of child sacrifice in the Valley of Hinnom (see 2 Kings 23:10). "For the king" translates *le-melekh*, which may also be read "for Molech". "The breath of the LORD" translates *nishmat YHWH*.
- **31:1** "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **31:2** "Wise" translates *chakham*.
- **31:3** "Human" translates *adam*, "God" translates *El*, "flesh" translates *basar*, and "spirit" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **31:4** "Growls" translates *yehgeh*.
- **31:5** "Hovering" translates *afot*. "Spare" translates *pasoach*, which is the verb of the passover (see Exodus 12:13).
- **31:6** "Turn" translates *shuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **31:8** "A sword not of man" translates *cherev lo-ish*.
- **31:9** "His rock" translates *sal'o*, and "the standard" translates *nes*.
- **32:1** "Will reign in righteousness" translates *le-tsedeq yimlokh-melekh*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "In justice" translates *le-mishpat*.
- **32:3** "Will not be closed" translates *tish'ena*, "will not be smeared over".
- **32:4** "The hasty" translates *nimharim*, and "the stammerers" translates *illegim*.
- **32:5** "The fool" translates *naval*, and "the scoundrel" translates *kilai*, a rare word.
- **32:6** "Folly" translates *nevalah*, "ungodliness" translates *chonef*, and "error" translates *to'ah*.
- **32:7** "His devices" translates *keliv*, a rare word.
- **32:8** "Noble" translates *nadiv*.
- **32:9** "Women who are at ease" translates *nashim sha'ananot*, and "complacent daughters" translates *banot botechot*.
- **32:11** "Strip" translates *pashotah*.
- **32:12** "Beat your breasts" translates *sofedim al-shadayim*, which is hard.
- **32:14** "The palace" translates *armon*, "the hill" translates *ofel*, and "the watchtower" translates *bachan*. "Dens" translates *me'arot*.
- **32:15** "The Spirit is poured out on us from on high" translates *ye'areh alenu ruach mi-marom*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **32:16** "Justice" translates *mishpat*, and "righteousness" translates *tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **32:17** "The effect of righteousness will be peace" translates *ve-hayah ma'aseh ha-tsedaqah shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Quietness and trust" translates *hashqet va-betach*.
- **32:19** "It will hail when the forest falls down" is hard in the Hebrew.
- **32:20** "Who let the ox and the donkey range free" translates *meshallechei regel ha-shor ve-ha-chamor*.
- **33:1** "The destroyer" translates *shoded*, and "traitor" translates *boged*.
- **33:2** "Be our arm" follows the Dead Sea scroll and the versions. The Hebrew text has *zero'am*, "their arm". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **33:3** "The tumultuous noise" translates *qol hamon*, and "when you lift yourself up" translates *me-rommemutekha*.
- **33:4** "The caterpillar" translates *chasil*, and "locusts" translates *gevim*. The verse is hard.
- **33:5** "Is exalted" translates *nisgav*.
- **33:6** "The stability of your times" translates *emunat ittekha*, and "abundance" translates *chosen*.
- **33:7** "Their heroes" translates *er'ellam*, which is hard and may be a name. "The envoys of peace" translates *mal'akhei shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **33:8** "Covenants" translates *berit*. See entry H-BERIT. "Cities" translates *arim*, which follows the Hebrew text most often read. The Dead Sea scroll has *edim*, "witnesses".
- **33:11** "Chaff" translates *chashash*, and "stubble" translates *qash*. "Your breath is a fire" translates *ruchakhem esh*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **33:12** "Burned to lime" translates *misrefot sid*.
- **33:14** "The godless" translates *chanefim*. "The consuming fire" translates *esh okhelah*, and "everlasting burnings" translates *moqedei olam*.
- **33:15** "Walks righteously" translates *holekh tsedaqot*.
- **33:17** "The king in his beauty" translates *melekh be-yofyo*, and "a land that stretches afar" translates *erets marchaqim*.
- **33:18** "The one who counted" translates *sofer*, and "weighed the tribute" translates *shoqel*.
- **33:19** "Of an obscure speech" translates *nif'ag safah*, a rare phrase.
- **33:20** "Our appointed feasts" translates *mo'adenu*, and "an immovable tent" translates *ohel bal-yitsan*.
- **33:21** "A galley with oars" translates *oni shayit*, and "a stately ship" translates *tsi addir*.
- **33:22** "Our judge ... our lawgiver ... our king" translates *shofetenu ... mechoqqenu ... malkenu*.
- **33:23** "Your cords hang loose" translates *nittshu chavalayikh*, which is hard. The ship may be Jerusalem.
- **33:24** "Will be forgiven their iniquity" translates *nesu avon*.
- **34:2** "Has devoted them to destruction" translates *hecherimam*, the verb of the ban.
- **34:4** "Will rot away" translates *nammaqu*.
- **34:5** "Has drunk its fill" translates *rivvetah*. "The people I have devoted to destruction" translates *am cheremi*.
- **34:6** "Bozrah" is a city of Edom.
- **34:7** "Wild oxen" translates *re'emim*.
- **34:8** "A day of vengeance, a year of recompense" translates *yom naqam shenat shillumim*. It is rendered as written, with no softening and no comment. See also 61:2 and 63:4.
- **34:9** "Pitch" translates *zefet*, and "sulphur" translates *gofrit*.
- **34:10** "Its smoke will go up for ever" is echoed in Revelation 14:11 and 19:3.
- **34:11** "The hawk" translates *qa'at*, "the porcupine" translates *qippod*, "the owl" translates *yanshuf*, and "the raven" translates *oreb*. The words are uncertain. "The line of confusion ... the plumb line of emptiness" translates *qav tohu ... avnei vohu*.
- **34:12** "Its nobles" translates *chorehah*, which is hard.
- **34:13** "Jackals" translates *tannim*, and "ostriches" translates *benot ya'anah*.
- **34:14** "Hyenas" translates *iyyim*, and "the wild goat" translates *sa'ir*. "The night creature" translates *lilit*, a rare word that may be the name of a demon of the night, and the footnote does not decide. "Settles" translates *hirgia*.
- **34:15** "The owl" translates *qippoz*, a rare word.
- **34:16** "The book of the LORD" translates *sefer YHWH*. "His Spirit has gathered them" translates *ruacho hu qibbetsan*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **34:17** "He has cast the lot" translates *hippil goral*.
- **35:1** "The meadow flower" translates *chavatselet*, as in the Song of Songs 2:1.
- **35:2** "The majesty" translates *hadar*.
- **35:3** "The weak hands" translates *yadayim rafot*, and "the feeble knees" translates *birkayim kosheloth*.
- **35:4** "An anxious heart" translates *nimharei-lev*. "Vengeance" translates *naqam*, and "recompense" translates *gemul*.
- **35:5** The verses 5 and 6 are echoed in Matthew 11:5 and Luke 7:22.
- **35:7** "The burning sand" translates *sharav*, and "the haunt of jackals" translates *neveh tannim*.
- **35:8** "A highway" translates *maslul*. "The Way of Holiness" translates *derekh ha-qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Even if they are fools, they will not go astray" translates *ve-evilim lo yit'u*, which is hard and may be read "fools will not wander on it".
- **35:9** "The redeemed" translates *ge'ulim*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **35:10** "The ransomed of the LORD" translates *pedu'ei YHWH*, as in 51:11. "Will return" translates *yeshuvun*. See entry H-SHUV. "Will flee away" translates *nasu*.
- **36:1** "The fourteenth year of King Hezekiah" is 701 before Christ. The verse is nearly the same as 2 Kings 18:13. The tribute and the stripping of the temple (2 Kings 18:14-16) are not in Isaiah.
- **36:2** "The Rabshakeh" translates *Rav-shaqeh*, the title of an Assyrian officer. "The conduit of the upper pool ... the Washer's Field" is as in 7:3.
- **36:3** "Eliakim ... Shebna the secretary ... Joah the recorder" are as in 22:15-20 and 2 Kings 18:18. Shebna is now the secretary (*sofer*), and Eliakim is over the household.
- **36:4** "The great king" translates *ha-melekh ha-gadol*, a title of the Assyrian kings.
- **36:5** "Strategy and power for war" translates *etsah u-gevurah la-milchamah*, and "mere words" translates *devar-sefatayim*. The first verb is *amarta* ("you say") or *amarti* ("I say"), and the text is hard.
- **36:6** "That broken reed of a staff" translates *mish'enet ha-qaneh ha-ratsuts ha-zeh*.
- **36:7** "Whose high places and altars Hezekiah has removed" refers to 2 Kings 18:4.
- **36:8** "Make a wager" translates *hit'arev*.
- **36:11** "Aramaic" translates *Aramit*, the language of diplomacy, and "the language of Judah" translates *Yehudit*.
- **36:12** "Their own dung ... their own urine" translates *choreihem ... shenehem*, which the reading tradition softens to polite words. The WABV text gives the plain words, as in the written text.
- **36:16** "Make a blessing with me" translates *asu itti berakhah*, which is hard and may be read "make peace with me" or "come to terms with me".
- **36:19** "Hamath and Arpad ... Sepharvaim" are places that the Assyrians took, as in 10:9.
- **36:21** "They were silent" translates *vayyacharishu*.
- **37:1** The verse is nearly the same as 2 Kings 19:1.
- **37:2** "The senior priests" translates *ziqnei ha-kohanim*.
- **37:3** "Children have come to the point of birth, and there is no strength to bring them forth" is a proverb of a crisis.
- **37:4** "The living God" translates *Elohim chai*, and "the remnant" translates *she'erit*.
- **37:6** "The young men" translates *ne'arei*.
- **37:7** "A spirit" translates *ruach*, and may mean a disposition or a rumour-bearing breath. See entry H-RUACH. "A rumour" translates *shemu'ah*.
- **37:8** "Libnah" is a city in Judah.
- **37:9** "Tirhakah king of Cush" translates *Tirhaqah melekh Kush*, an Egyptian king of the twenty-fifth dynasty.
- **37:12** "Gozan, Haran, Rezeph, and the people of Eden who were in Tel-assar" are places in Mesopotamia that the Assyrians took.
- **37:14** "The letter" translates *ha-sefarim*, a plural form that is also used for one letter.
- **37:16** "Enthroned above the cherubim" translates *yoshev ha-keruvim*, as in Psalm 80:1 and 99:1.
- **37:18** "All the nations and their lands" follows 2 Kings 19:17 and the Dead Sea scroll. The Hebrew text of Isaiah has *ha-aratsot ve-et artsam*, "all the lands and their land". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew of Isaiah here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **37:19** "Not gods" translates *lo elohim*, and "the work of human hands" translates *ma'aseh yedei adam*. See also 44:9-20.
- **37:20** "That you alone are the LORD" translates *attah YHWH levaddekha*.
- **37:22** "The young daughter of Zion" translates *betulat bat-Tsiyyon*, in which "virgin daughter" is a figure of the city. "She wags her head" translates *henia rosh*.
- **37:23** "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **37:24** "The heights of the mountains" translates *merom harim*, "the far recesses of Lebanon" translates *yarketei Levanon*, and "cypresses" translates *berosh*.
- **37:25** "I dug wells" translates *qarti*. "The streams of Egypt" translates *ye'orei matsor*, in which *matsor* is a name for Egypt. The words are as in 2 Kings 19:24, where the Hebrew has "in foreign lands" as well.
- **37:26** "Fortified cities crash into heaps of ruins" translates *le-hash'ot gallim nitsim arim betsurot*.
- **37:27** "Shorn of strength" translates *qitsrei-yad*, and "blighted" translates *shedefah*.
- **37:29** "Your complacency" translates *sha'ananekha*, "my hook in your nose" translates *chachi be-apekha*, and "my bit" translates *mitgi*.
- **37:30** "What grows of itself" translates *safiach*, and "what springs from that" translates *sachish*, two rare words for crops that grow without sowing.
- **37:31** "The surviving remnant" translates *pelitat*, and "take root downward and bear fruit upward" translates *shoresh le-matta ve-asah peri le-ma'lah*.
- **37:32** "The zeal of the LORD of hosts" is as in 9:7. In 2 Kings 19:31 the Hebrew has "the zeal of the LORD".
- **37:35** "I will defend this city" translates *ganoti al-ha-ir*.
- **37:36** "The angel of the LORD" translates *mal'ak YHWH*. The number 185,000 is as in 2 Kings 19:35. The verse is rendered as written.
- **37:37** "Nineveh" is the capital of Assyria.
- **37:38** "Nisroch" is the name of a god. "Adrammelech and Sharezer" are sons of Sennacherib, and "Esarhaddon" is the son who followed him (in 681 before Christ). "Ararat" is the land of Urartu.
- **38:1** "In those days" translates *ba-yamim ha-hem*. "Set your house in order" translates *tsav le-veitekha*, and "you will die" translates *met attah*. The story is as in 2 Kings 20:1-11, with Hezekiah's writing added.
- **38:3** "With a whole heart" translates *be-lev shalem*, in which *shalem* is the adjective "whole" and is not tagged. "In faithfulness" translates *be-emet*.
- **38:5** "Fifteen years" is as in 2 Kings 20:6.
- **38:8** "The dial of Ahaz" translates *ma'alot Achaz*, and "steps" translates *ma'alot*, which may mean "steps" or "degrees". The shadow is made to go back ten steps. The order of the verses 21 and 22 follows the Hebrew of Isaiah, so that the poultice is told at the end of the chapter, after the writing. In 2 Kings 20:7-8 it is told before the sign.
- **38:9** "A writing" translates *mikhtav*.
- **38:10** "In the middle of my days" translates *bi-demi yamai*. "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL. "I am consigned" translates *puqqadti*.
- **38:11** "Yah, Yah" translates *Yah Yah*, the short form of the divine name, repeated. "Humankind" translates *adam*, and "the world" translates *cheled*, a rare word.
- **38:12** "My dwelling" translates *dori*, which is hard and may mean "my generation". "Like a weaver" translates *ka-orreg*, and "the loom" translates *dallah*.
- **38:13** "I calmed myself" translates *shivviti*, which is a rare form and the text is hard.
- **38:14** "A swallow" translates *sus*, and "a crane" translates *agur*. "Be my pledge of safety" translates *arvani*, "be surety for me".
- **38:15** "The bitterness of my soul" translates *mar nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **38:16** "The life of my spirit" translates *chayyei ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH. The verse is hard.
- **38:17** "My welfare" translates *le-shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Great bitterness" translates *mar li mar*, and "the pit of destruction" translates *shachat beli*. "You have cast all my sins behind your back" is echoed in Micah 7:19.
- **38:18** "Sheol does not thank you" is rendered as written, with no softening. See also Psalm 6:5, 88:10-12 and 115:17. "The pit" translates *bor*.
- **38:20** "Stringed instruments" translates *neginotai*.
- **38:21** "A cake of figs" translates *develet te'enim*, and "the boil" translates *ha-shechin*.
- **39:1** "Merodach-baladan" translates *Merodach Baladan*, a king of Babylon who opposed Assyria.
- **39:2** "Welcomed them gladly" translates *vayyismach aleihem*. "His treasure house" translates *beit nekhoto*, and "his whole armoury" translates *beit kelav*.
- **39:7** "Eunuchs" translates *sarisim*, which may also mean "officials".
- **39:8** "Peace and security" translates *shalom ve-emet*, "peace and truth". See entry H-SHALOM.
- **40:1** "Comfort, comfort" translates *nachamu nachamu*, a plural command to the heralds. See entry H-NACHAM. "My people" translates *ammi*.
- **40:2** "Speak tenderly" translates *dabberu al-lev*, "speak to the heart". "Her warfare" translates *tseva'ah*, which may mean "her hard service" or "her term". "Is pardoned" translates *nirtsah avonah*, "her iniquity is paid". "Double" translates *kiflayim*.
- **40:3** "A voice cries" translates *qol qore*. In the Hebrew the words "in the wilderness" belong to "prepare the way of the LORD", and the Greek of Matthew 3:3, Mark 1:3, Luke 3:4 and John 1:23 joins them to the voice ("a voice crying in the wilderness"). "A highway" translates *mesillah*.
- **40:5** "The glory of the LORD" translates *kevod YHWH*.
- **40:6** "A voice says" translates *qol omer*, and the Dead Sea scroll has "and I said". "Its beauty" translates *chasdo*, which is the word *chesed* and may mean "its constancy" or "its loveliness". It is quoted in 1 Peter 1:24 as "glory".
- **40:7** "The breath of the LORD" translates *ruach YHWH*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **40:9** "Herald of good news" translates *mevasseret*, a feminine form that may be read "herald to Zion" or "Zion, herald".
- **40:10** "His arm" translates *zero'o*, "his reward" translates *sekhaaro*, and "his recompense" translates *pe'ullato*.
- **40:11** "Those that are with young" translates *alot*.
- **40:12** "The hollow of his hand" translates *sho'al*, "a span" translates *zeret*, and "a measure" translates *shalish*, a rare word.
- **40:13** "The Spirit of the LORD" translates *ruach YHWH*. See entry H-RUACH. "Has measured" translates *tikken*. The verse is quoted in Romans 11:34 and 1 Corinthians 2:16.
- **40:14** "The path of justice" translates *orach mishpat*.
- **40:15** "A drop from a bucket" translates *mar mi-deli*.
- **40:17** "Emptiness" translates *tohu*.
- **40:19** "An idol" translates *ha-pesel*. The verses 19 and 20 are hard in the Hebrew, and the words *ha-mesukkan terumah* are not clear.
- **40:22** "The circle of the earth" translates *chug ha-arets*, and "grasshoppers" translates *chagavim*. "A curtain" translates *doq*.
- **40:24** "He blows on them" translates *nashaf bahem*.
- **40:25** "The Holy One" translates *qadosh*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **40:26** "By the greatness of his might" translates *me-rov onim*, and "strong in power" translates *ammits koach*.
- **40:27** "My way is hidden from the LORD" translates *nistrah darki me-YHWH*, and "my right is disregarded" translates *mishpati me-Elohai ya'avor*.
- **40:28** "The everlasting God" translates *Elohei olam*, and "unsearchable" translates *ein cheqer*.
- **40:31** "Those who wait for the LORD" translates *qovei YHWH*. "Will renew their strength" translates *yachalifu koach*. "Will mount up with wings like eagles" translates *ya'alu ever ka-nesharim*, which is hard and may be read "will grow pinions like eagles".
- **41:1** "Coastlands" translates *iyyim*.
- **41:2** "One from the east" refers to Cyrus (see 45:1). "Victory" translates *tsedeq*, in the phrase *tsedeq yiqra'ehu le-raglo*, "righteousness calls him to its foot", which may be read "victory meets him at every step". See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **41:4** "I, the LORD, the first, and with the last. I am he" translates *ani YHWH rishon ve-et acharonim ani hu*.
- **41:7** "The soldering" translates *ha-devek*.
- **41:8** "My friend" translates *ohavi*, "my lover", which is said of Abraham (see also James 2:23).
- **41:10** "My righteous right hand" translates *bi-yemin tsidqi*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **41:14** "You worm Jacob" translates *tola'at Ya'aqov*. "You people of Israel" translates *metei Yisra'el*, in which *metei* is rare and may mean "few". "Your Redeemer" translates *go'alekh*. See entry H-GOEL. "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **41:15** "A threshing sledge" translates *morag*.
- **41:19** "The cedar, the acacia, the myrtle and the olive ... the cypress, the plane and the pine" translates *erez shittah vehadas ve-ets shemen ... berosh tidhar u-te'ashshur*. The names of the trees are uncertain.
- **41:21** "The King of Jacob" translates *melekh Ya'aqov*.
- **41:25** "He will call on my name" translates *yiqra bi-shmi*. The Dead Sea scroll has "he will call my name".
- **41:27** "Behold, here they are" is hard in the Hebrew (*hinneh hinnemo*).
- **41:29** "Empty wind" translates *ruach va-tohu*.
- **42:1** "My servant" translates *avdi*, "whom I uphold" translates *etmakh-bo*, and "my chosen" translates *bechiri*. "My soul delights" translates *ratsetah nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "My Spirit" translates *ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH. "Justice" translates *mishpat*. The verses 1 to 4 are quoted in Matthew 12:18-21 in a Greek form.
- **42:3** "A faintly burning wick" translates *pishtah kehah*. "Faithfully" translates *le-emet*.
- **42:4** "Will not grow faint or be discouraged" translates *lo yichheh ve-lo yarutz*, in which *yarutz* means "be crushed" (the same verb as *ratsats* in verse 3). "His teaching" translates *torato*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **42:5** "Breath" translates *neshamah*, and "spirit" translates *ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **42:6** "In righteousness" translates *be-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "A covenant for the people" translates *berit am*. See entry H-BERIT. The word *am* may mean Israel or the nations. "A light for the nations" translates *or goyim*, and is echoed in Luke 2:32 and Acts 13:47.
- **42:7** "Blind" translates *iwwerot*.
- **42:8** "My glory" translates *kevodi*, and "my praise" translates *tehillati*.
- **42:10** "A new song" translates *shir chadash*, as in Psalm 96:1 and 98:1.
- **42:11** "Kedar" is the desert tribes, and "Sela" is a rock city in Edom.
- **42:13** "His zeal" translates *qin'ah*.
- **42:14** "I have held my peace" translates *hecheshiti*.
- **42:15** "Islands" translates *iyyim*, the same word as "coastlands" in 42:10.
- **42:19** "My dedicated one" translates *meshullam*, which is hard and may mean "my perfected one", "my friend", "the one at peace" or "the one who is paid".
- **42:20** "He sees many things, but does not observe them" follows the reading tradition (*ra'ot* ... *lo yishmor*). The written text has the second person ("you see"). The verse is hard.
- **42:21** "His righteousness" translates *tsidqo*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "His teaching" translates *torah*. See entry H-TORAH. "Magnify" translates *yagdil*.
- **42:22** "Trapped in holes" translates *hafeach ba-chururim*, and "prisons" translates *beit kela'im*.
- **42:24** "The looter" translates *li-meshissah*, "the plunderers" translates *le-vozezim*, and "whose teaching they did not obey" translates *ve-lo shamu be-torato*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **42:25** "The heat of his anger" translates *chemah appo*.
- **43:1** "He who created you" translates *boreakha*, "he who formed you" translates *yotsercha*, and "I have redeemed you" translates *ge'alticha*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **43:3** "Your Saviour" translates *moshi'ekha*. "Your ransom" translates *kofrekha*. See entry H-KAPPER. "Seba" translates *Seva*, a land in Africa.
- **43:4** "You are precious" translates *yaqarta*. "I give people in return for you" translates *ve-eten adam tachtekha*, and "your life" translates *nafshekha*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **43:7** "Who is called by my name" translates *ha-niqra vi-shmi*.
- **43:8** "Bring out the people who are blind, yet have eyes" translates *hotsi am iwwer ve-yesh einayim*.
- **43:9** "Their witnesses" translates *edeihem*.
- **43:10** "You are my witnesses" translates *attem edai*. "Was formed" translates *notsar*.
- **43:11** "Saviour" translates *moshia*.
- **43:12** "A strange god" translates *zar*.
- **43:14** "Your Redeemer" translates *go'alekhem*. See entry H-GOEL. "Fugitives" translates *bariachim*. "The Chaldeans, in the ships in which they rejoice" translates *Kasdim ba-oniyyot rinnatam*, which is hard and may be read "the Chaldeans, whose shout is in the ships".
- **43:15** "Your Holy One" translates *qedoshekhem*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The Creator of Israel" translates *bore Yisra'el*.
- **43:19** "A new thing" translates *chadashah*. See also Revelation 21:5.
- **43:20** "The wild beasts ... the jackals and the ostriches" translates *chayyat ha-sadeh tannim u-venot ya'anah*.
- **43:22** "Have been weary of me" translates *yagata bi*, which is hard.
- **43:24** "Sweet cane" translates *qaneh*, a spice (see Jeremiah 6:20). "Satisfied me" translates *rivvitani*.
- **43:25** "Blots out" translates *mocheh*. The verse is as in 44:22.
- **43:26** "Let us argue together" translates *nishshafetah yachad*, a word of the court.
- **43:27** "Your first father" translates *avikha ha-rishon*, which may refer to Adam, Jacob or Abraham. "Your mediators" translates *melitsekha*, which may mean "your interpreters" or "your spokesmen", and is hard.
- **43:28** "I profaned the princes of the sanctuary" translates *va-achallel sarei qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Utter destruction" translates *cherem*, and "reviling" translates *gidduphim*.
- **44:2** "Jeshurun" translates *Yeshurun*, a poetic name of Israel (see Deuteronomy 32:15 and 33:5). "Who formed you from the womb" translates *yotsercha mi-vaten*.
- **44:3** "I will pour" translates *etsoq*. "My Spirit" translates *ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH. "Your offspring" translates *zar'ekha*.
- **44:4** "Willows" translates *aravim*.
- **44:5** "Will write on his hand" translates *yiktov yado*, which may refer to a mark or a tattoo. "Name himself" translates *yekhanneh*.
- **44:6** "His Redeemer" translates *go'alo*. See entry H-GOEL. "I am the first and I am the last" translates *ani rishon va-ani acharon*, and is echoed in Revelation 1:17 and 22:13.
- **44:7** "Who is like me?" translates *mi khamoni*. "Since I appointed an ancient people" translates *mi-sumi am olam*, which is hard.
- **44:8** "Rock" translates *tsur*.
- **44:9** "Fashion" translates *yotserei*, and "the things they delight in" translates *chamuddeihem*.
- **44:12** "A cutting tool" translates *ma'atsad*, a rare word. The verse is hard in the Hebrew.
- **44:13** "A pencil" translates *sered*, "planes" translates *maqtsu'ot*, and "a compass" translates *mechugah*.
- **44:14** "Cypress" translates *tirzah*, "oak" translates *allon*, and "cedar" translates *oren*, a rare word that may mean "ash" or "laurel".
- **44:18** "He has shut" translates *tach*, "has smeared over".
- **44:19** "A block of wood" translates *bud ets*.
- **44:20** "He feeds on ashes" translates *ro'eh efer*, and "a deluded heart" translates *lev hothal*. "Is there not a lie in my right hand?" translates *halo sheqer bi-yemini*.
- **44:22** "I have blotted out" translates *machiti*. "Return" translates *shuvah*. See entry H-SHUV. "I have redeemed you" translates *ge'alticha*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **44:23** "The depths of the earth" translates *tachtiyyot arets*.
- **44:24** "Your Redeemer" translates *go'alekha*. See entry H-GOEL. "By myself" follows the reading tradition (*me-iti*). The written text has a different form (*mi itti*, "who was with me?").
- **44:25** "The signs" translates *otot*, "liars" translates *baddim*, and "diviners" translates *qosemim*.
- **44:27** "The deep" translates *metsulah*.
- **44:28** "Cyrus" translates *Koresh*, who ruled Persia and took Babylon in 539 before Christ. "My shepherd" translates *ro'i*, and "the temple" translates *heikhal*. "Your foundation will be laid" translates *tivvased*.
- **45:1** "His anointed" translates *li-meshicho*. See entry H-MASHIACH. The word is used here of a foreign king. "To loose the loins of kings" translates *u-fitchei melakhim aftach*.
- **45:2** "The exalted places" translates *hadurim*. The Dead Sea scroll has *harrim*, "mountains".
- **45:3** "The treasures of darkness" translates *otserot choshekh*, and "the hoards in secret places" translates *matmonei mistarim*.
- **45:4** "I name you" translates *akhanneka*.
- **45:5** "I equip you" translates *a'azzerkha*, "I gird you".
- **45:7** "I form light and create darkness" translates *yotser or u-vore choshekh*. "I make well-being and create calamity" translates *oseh shalom u-vore ra*. See entry H-SHALOM. The word *ra* means "evil" or "calamity". The verse is rendered as written, with no softening. See also Amos 3:6 and Lamentations 3:38.
- **45:8** "Righteousness" translates *tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Salvation" translates *yesha*.
- **45:9** "A pot among earthen pots" translates *cheres et-charsei adamah*, and "your work has no handles" translates *ve-fo'alekha ein yadayim lo*. The verses 9 and 10 are echoed in Romans 9:20-21.
- **45:11** "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Ask me of things to come" translates *ha-otiyyot sha'aluni*, which is hard.
- **45:13** "I have stirred him up" translates *he'iroti-hu*. "In righteousness" translates *be-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "For price or reward" translates *lo vi-mechir ve-lo vi-shochad*.
- **45:14** "The wealth of Egypt" translates *yegia Mitsrayim*, and "the Sabeans, people of stature" translates *Seva'im anshei middah*. "In chains" translates *ba-zikkim*.
- **45:15** "A God who hides himself" translates *El mistattér*.
- **45:17** "Everlasting salvation" translates *teshu'at olamim*.
- **45:18** "He did not create it empty" translates *lo-tohu vera'ah*, and "he formed it to be inhabited" translates *la-shevet yetsarah*. See Genesis 1:2.
- **45:19** "Seek me in vain" translates *tohu baqqeshuni*.
- **45:20** "Survivors" translates *pelitei*.
- **45:21** "A righteous God and a Saviour" translates *El tsaddiq u-moshia*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **45:22** "Turn to me" translates *pnu elai*. See entry H-SHUV. "Be saved" translates *hivvashe'u*.
- **45:23** "By myself I have sworn" translates *bi nishba'ti*. "Every knee will bow, every tongue will swear allegiance" is quoted in Romans 14:11 and Philippians 2:10-11 in the Greek form.
- **45:24** "Only in the LORD, it will be said of me, are righteousness and strength" translates *akh ba-YHWH li amar tsedaqot va-oz*, which is hard. The words *li amar* may be read "it shall be said of me" or "he has said to me".
- **45:25** "Will be justified" translates *yitstsedequ*.
- **46:1** "Bel" and "Nebo" are Marduk and Nabu, the great gods of Babylon. "Their idols are on beasts" translates *hayu atsabbeihem la-chayyah*. "These things you carry are borne as burdens on weary beasts" translates *nesu'otekhem amusot massa la'ayefah*, which is hard.
- **46:2** "Captivity" translates *shevi*.
- **46:3** "Who have been borne by me from before your birth" translates *ha-amusim mini-vaten*.
- **46:4** "Your old age" translates *ziqnah*, and "grey hairs" translates *seivah*. "I will carry" translates *esbol*.
- **46:6** "Those who lavish gold" translates *ha-zallim zahav*, in which *zallim* is a rare word.
- **46:8** "Stand firm" translates *hit'oshashu*, a rare verb that may mean "be men" or "stand firm". "Transgressors" translates *poshe'im*.
- **46:11** "A bird of prey from the east" translates *ayit mi-mizrach*, a figure of Cyrus (see 41:2 and 45:1).
- **46:12** "You stubborn of heart" translates *abbirei lev*, and "far from righteousness" translates *rechoqim mi-tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **46:13** "My righteousness" translates *tsidqati*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **47:1** "Virgin daughter of Babylon" translates *betulat bat-Bavel*, a figure of the city. "Tender and delicate" translates *rakkah va-anugah*.
- **47:2** "Millstones" translates *rechayim*, "your veil" translates *tsammatekh*, "your robe" translates *shovel*, a rare word, and "your legs" translates *shoq*.
- **47:3** "Your nakedness" translates *ervatekh*. "I will spare no one" translates *lo epga adam*, which is hard and may be read "I will not meet a man" or "I will not intercede for anyone".
- **47:4** "Our Redeemer" translates *go'alenu*. See entry H-GOEL. "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH. The verse is a cry of praise within the taunt.
- **47:6** "I profaned my heritage" translates *challalti nachalati*, and "mercy" translates *rachamim*.
- **47:7** "Mistress for ever" translates *gevirat ad*.
- **47:8** "Lover of pleasures" translates *adinah*, and "securely" translates *la-betach*. "I am, and there is no one besides me" translates *ani ve-afsi od*, the same claim that the LORD makes of himself in 45:5. "The loss of children" translates *shekhol*.
- **47:9** "Your sorceries" translates *keshafayikh*, and "your enchantments" translates *chavarayikh*.
- **47:11** "Charm away" translates *shachrah*, a rare word. "You will not be able to atone for" translates *lo tukhli kapperah*. See entry H-KAPPER. "Ruin" translates *sho'ah*.
- **47:13** "Those who divide the heavens" translates *hovrei shamayim*, "the dividers of the sky", the astrologers.
- **47:14** "No coal for warming oneself" translates *ein gachelet la-chammotam*.
- **47:15** "They wander about" translates *ta'u*.
- **48:1** "Who came from the waters of Judah" translates *u-mi-mei Yehudah yatsa'u*. The Dead Sea scroll has *u-mi-meei*, "from the loins of Judah". "Not in truth or right" translates *lo be-emet ve-lo bi-tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **48:2** "The holy city" translates *ir ha-qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **48:4** "Obstinate" translates *qashah*, "an iron sinew" translates *gid barzel*, and "brass" translates *nechushah*.
- **48:8** "You would deal very treacherously" translates *bagod tivgod*, and "a rebel" translates *poshea*.
- **48:9** "I defer my anger" translates *a'arikh appi*.
- **48:10** "But not as silver" translates *lo ve-khasef*, which is hard and may be read "not for silver" or "in no way like silver".
- **48:11** "For how should my name be profaned" follows the Dead Sea scroll and the versions, in which the word "my name" is supplied. The Hebrew text has *ki eikh yechal*, "for how should it be profaned". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **48:14** "The LORD loves him" refers to Cyrus (see 41:2 and 45:1).
- **48:16** "The Lord GOD has sent me, and his Spirit" translates *Adonai YHWH shelachani ve-ruacho*. See entry H-RUACH. The speaker may be the prophet or the servant, and the words *ve-ruacho* may be read "with his Spirit". The verse is hard.
- **48:17** "Your Redeemer" translates *go'alekha*. See entry H-GOEL. "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Who teaches you to profit" translates *melammedekha le-ho'il*.
- **48:18** "Your peace" translates *shelomekha*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Your righteousness" translates *tsidqatekha*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **48:20** "Go out from Babylon" translates *tse'u mi-Bavel*, and is echoed in Jeremiah 50:8, 51:6 and 51:45 and in Revelation 18:4. "Has redeemed" translates *ga'al*.
- **48:22** "No peace" translates *ein shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. The verse is the same as 57:21.
- **49:1** "The LORD called me from the womb" translates *YHWH mi-beten qeraani*, and "he named my name" translates *hizkir shemi*. The speaker is the servant.
- **49:2** "A polished arrow" translates *chets barur*.
- **49:3** "You are my servant, Israel" translates *avdi attah Yisra'el*. The servant is called Israel here. "In whom I will be glorified" translates *asher-bekha et-pa'ar*.
- **49:4** "In vain" translates *la-riq*, and "nothing and vanity" translates *le-tohu ve-hevel*.
- **49:5** "To bring Jacob back" translates *le-shovev Ya'aqov*. "That Israel might be gathered to him" follows the reading tradition (*lo ye'asef*). The written text has *lo*, "not", so that it reads "and Israel is not gathered".
- **49:6** "It is too light a thing" translates *nqal mi-heyotkha*. "The preserved of Israel" translates *netsirei Yisra'el*. "A light for the nations" translates *or goyim*, as in 42:6, and it is quoted in Acts 13:47.
- **49:7** "The Redeemer of Israel" translates *go'el Yisra'el*. See entry H-GOEL. "His Holy One" translates *qedosho*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Deeply despised" translates *bezoh nefesh*, which is hard and may be read "despised in soul". "Abhorred by the nation" translates *meta'av goy*, and "the servant of rulers" translates *eved moshelim*.
- **49:8** "A time of favour" translates *et ratson*. "I will keep you" translates *etsrekha*. "A covenant to the people" translates *li-vrit am*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **49:9** "Bare heights" translates *shefayim*.
- **49:10** "Scorching wind" translates *sharav*, and "the one who has pity on them" translates *merachamam*.
- **49:12** "The land of Sinim" translates *erets Sinim*. The place is not known. Some read it as Syene (Aswan) in southern Egypt, as the Dead Sea scroll suggests, and others as a far land to the east.
- **49:13** "Has comforted" translates *nicham*. See entry H-NACHAM. "His afflicted" translates *aniyyav*, and "will have compassion" translates *yerachem*, from *racham*.
- **49:15** "Her nursing child" translates *ulah*, and "compassion" translates *racheim*.
- **49:16** "I have engraved you" translates *chaqqotikh*.
- **49:17** "Your builders" translates *banayikh*, which may be read "your builders" or "your sons".
- **49:18** "An ornament" translates *adi*, and "a bride" translates *kallah*.
- **49:21** "Put away" translates *sura*.
- **49:22** "My signal" translates *nissi*, and "in their arms" translates *ba-chotsen*.
- **49:23** "Foster fathers" translates *omenim*, and "nursing mothers" translates *meniqotayikh*.
- **49:24** "The captives of a tyrant" follows the Dead Sea scroll, the Syriac and the Latin (*shevi aritz*). The Hebrew text has *shevi tsaddiq*, "the captives of the righteous". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **49:25** "The mighty" translates *gibbor*, and "contend" translates *yariv*.
- **49:26** "Eat their own flesh ... drunk with their own blood" is rendered as written. "Your Saviour" translates *moshi'ekh*, "your Redeemer" translates *go'alekh* (see entry H-GOEL), and "the Mighty One of Jacob" translates *avir Ya'aqov*.
- **50:1** "Your mother's certificate of divorce" translates *sefer keritut immekhem*. "My creditors" translates *noshai*, and "you were sold" translates *nimkartem*.
- **50:2** "Is my hand shortened" translates *ha-qatsor qatsera yadi*, and "redeem" translates *pedut*.
- **50:3** "Blackness" translates *qadrut*.
- **50:4** "The tongue of those who are taught" translates *leshon limmudim*. "To sustain" translates *la'ut*, a rare verb. "Morning by morning" translates *ba-boqer ba-boqer*. This is the third servant song.
- **50:6** "Those who strike" translates *makkim*, "disgrace" translates *kelimmot*, and "spitting" translates *yoreq*.
- **50:7** "Flint" translates *challamish*.
- **50:8** "The one who vindicates me" translates *matsdiqi*, and "my adversary" translates *ba'al mishpati*.
- **50:9** "Declare me guilty" translates *yarshi'eni*.
- **50:10** "His servant" translates *avdo*.
- **50:11** "Who equip yourselves with burning torches" translates *me'azzerei zikot*, which is hard and may be read "who gird on firebrands". "Torment" translates *ma'atsevah*.
- **51:1** "Pursue righteousness" translates *rodefei tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "The quarry" translates *maqqevet bor*.
- **51:2** "Sarah" translates *Sarah*, and "he was but one" translates *echad qeraativ*.
- **51:3** "Comforts" translates *nicham*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **51:4** "A teaching" translates *torah*. See entry H-TORAH. "My justice" translates *mishpati*.
- **51:5** "My righteousness draws near" translates *qarov tsidqi*.
- **51:6** "Will vanish like smoke" translates *nimlechu*. "In like manner" translates *kemo-ken*, which may also be read "like gnats".
- **51:7** "My teaching" translates *torati*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **51:9** "The arm of the LORD" translates *zero'a YHWH*, "Rahab" translates *Rahav*, and "the dragon" translates *tannin*. See also 30:7 and Psalm 89:10.
- **51:10** "The redeemed" translates *ge'ulim*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **51:11** The verse is the same as 35:10, and the wording is matched. See entry H-SHUV.
- **51:12** "Comforts you" translates *menachemkhem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **51:13** "The oppressor" translates *ha-metsiq*.
- **51:14** "He who is bowed down" translates *tso'eh*, and "will be released" translates *yippatéach*.
- **51:17** "The cup of staggering" translates *qubbat kos ha-tar'elah*, in which *qubbat* is a rare word.
- **51:19** "Console you" translates *anachemekh*, from *nacham*. See entry H-NACHAM. The Dead Sea scroll and the versions read "who will comfort you".
- **51:20** "An antelope in a net" translates *te'o mikhmar*.
- **51:22** "Pleads the cause" translates *yariv riv*.
- **52:1** "The holy city" translates *ir ha-qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The uncircumcised" translates *arel*.
- **52:2** "Shake yourself from the dust" translates *hitnaari me-afar*, and "loose the bonds from your neck" follows the reading tradition (*hitpattechu*, "be loosed"). The written text has the command "loose yourself".
- **52:4** "For nothing" translates *be-efes*.
- **52:5** "Wail" translates *yehelilu*, and "is despised" translates *mitna'ats*.
- **52:6** "Here I am" translates *hineni*.
- **52:7** "How beautiful on the mountains" translates *mah-navu al-heharim*. "Brings good news" translates *mevasser*, and "publishes peace" translates *mashmia shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Your God reigns" translates *malakh Elohayikh*. The verse is quoted in Romans 10:15, and it is echoed in Nahum 1:15.
- **52:8** "Your watchmen" translates *tsofayikh*, "eye to eye" translates *ayin be-ayin*, and "the return" translates *be-shuv*.
- **52:9** "Has comforted" translates *nicham*. See entry H-NACHAM. "Has redeemed" translates *ga'al*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **52:10** "Has bared his holy arm" translates *chasaf YHWH et-zero'a qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **52:11** "You who bear the vessels of the LORD" translates *nose'ei kelei YHWH*, a reference to the temple vessels carried home from Babylon.
- **52:12** "Your rear guard" translates *me'assefkhem*, as in 58:8.
- **52:13** "My servant will act wisely" translates *yaskil avdi*, in which the verb may mean "act wisely" or "prosper". "High and lifted up" translates *yarum ve-nissa ve-gavah me'od*. The verse begins the fourth servant song (52:13 to 53:12).
- **52:14** "As many were astonished at you" translates *ka'asher shamemu alekha rabbim*, in which the Hebrew has "you", and the Dead Sea scroll and the Greek have "him". "His appearance was so marred, beyond human semblance" translates *mishchat me-ish mar'ehu*, which is hard.
- **52:15** "So he will sprinkle many nations" translates *ken yazzeh goyim rabbim*, in which *yazzeh* means "sprinkle", and some read it "startle" (as the Greek has "will astonish"). The second half is quoted in Romans 15:21.
- **53:1** "What he has heard from us" translates *li-shmu'atenu*. "Has been revealed" translates *nigletah*. The verse is quoted in John 12:38 and Romans 10:16.
- **53:2** "A young plant" translates *yoneq*, "a root out of dry ground" translates *shoresh me-erets tsiyyah*, "form" translates *to'ar*, "majesty" translates *hadar*, and "beauty" translates *mar'eh*.
- **53:3** "Despised" translates *nivzeh*. "Rejected by people" translates *chadal ishim*, in which *chadal* may mean "rejected", "lacking" or "ceasing". "A man of pains" translates *ish makh'ovot*, and "familiar with sickness" translates *yedua choli*. "One from whom people hide their faces" translates *ke-mastir panim mimmenu*, and "we did not value him" translates *lo chashavnuhu*.
- **53:4** "Has borne our sicknesses and carried our pains" translates *chole'enu hu nasa u-makh'ovenu sevalam*. It is quoted in Matthew 8:17. "Struck, beaten by God, and humbled" translates *nagua muke Elohim u-me'unneh*.
- **53:5** "Pierced" translates *mecholal*, and "crushed" translates *medukka*. "The discipline that brought us peace" translates *musar shelomenu*. See entry H-SHALOM. "His wounds" translates *chaburato*, and "we are healed" translates *nirpa-lanu*. The verse is echoed in 1 Peter 2:24.
- **53:6** "Have gone astray" translates *ta'inu*. "Has laid on him" translates *hifgia bo*, which may also mean "caused to meet him". The verse is echoed in 1 Peter 2:25.
- **53:7** "He was oppressed, and he was afflicted" translates *niggas ve-hu na'aneh*, and "a lamb" translates *seh*. The verse is quoted in Acts 8:32-33.
- **53:8** "By restraint and judgement" translates *me-otser u-me-mishpat*, which may be read "by oppression and judgement" or "from prison and from judgement". "Taken away" translates *luqqach*, and "his generation" translates *doro*. "Who considered" translates *yesochéach*. "Struck for the transgression of my people" translates *nega lamo*, in which *lamo* may mean "to them" or "to him", and the verse is hard.
- **53:9** "His grave was assigned" translates *va-yitten qivro*. "A rich man in his death" translates *ve-et ashir be-motav*, in which *be-motav* is a plural form ("in his deaths") and the Dead Sea scroll has a different word. The verse is hard.
- **53:10** "It was the LORD's will to crush him" translates *va-YHWH chafets deka'o*, and "he has put him to grief" translates *hecheli*. "When his soul makes a guilt offering" translates *im-tasim asham nafsho*, in which *tasim* may be read "you make" or "she (the soul) makes", and *asham* is the guilt offering. See entry H-NEPHESH. "He will see offspring" translates *yir'eh zera*. The verse is very hard.
- **53:11** "He will see and be satisfied" follows the Hebrew text. The Dead Sea scroll and the Greek have "he will see light and be satisfied". "By his knowledge" translates *be-da'to*, and "the righteous one, my servant, will make many to be accounted righteous" translates *yatsdiq tsaddiq avdi la-rabbim*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "He will bear" translates *yisbol*.
- **53:12** "I will give him a portion with the many" translates *achalleq-lo ba-rabbim*, in which *ba-rabbim* may also be read "among the great". "The strong" translates *atsumim*. "He poured out his soul" translates *he'erah la-mavet nafsho*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Was counted with the transgressors" translates *et-poshe'im nimna*, and is quoted in Luke 22:37. "He bore the sin of many" translates *chet-rabbim nasa*, and "interceded" translates *yafgia*.
- **54:1** "Barren one" translates *aqarah*, "the desolate one" translates *shomemah*, and "her who is married" translates *be'ulah*. The verse is echoed in Galatians 4:27.
- **54:2** "The curtains" translates *yeri'ot*, and "your stakes" translates *yetedotayikh*.
