- **54:3** "You will spread abroad" translates *tifrotsi*, and "will possess the nations" translates *goyim yirash*.
- **54:4** "The shame of your youth" translates *boshet alumayikh*, and "your widowhood" translates *almenut*.
- **54:5** "Your Maker is your husband" translates *ba'alayikh osayikh*, in which both words are plural forms of majesty. "Your Redeemer" translates *go'alekh*. See entry H-GOEL. "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **54:6** "Grieved in spirit" translates *atsuvat ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "A wife of youth" translates *ishet ne'urim*, and "cast off" translates *me'as*.
- **54:7** "Great compassion" translates *rachamim gedolim*.
- **54:8** "In overflowing anger" translates *be-shetsef qetsef*. "Everlasting steadfast love" translates *chesed olam*. See entry H-HESED.
- **54:9** "The days of Noah" follows the Dead Sea scroll and the versions. The Hebrew text has *mei Noach*, "the waters of Noah", which may be a play on the two senses of the word. The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar.
- **54:10** "My covenant of peace" translates *berit shelomi*. See entries H-BERIT and H-SHALOM. "My steadfast love" translates *chasdi*. See entry H-HESED.
- **54:11** "Storm-tossed" translates *so'arah*, and "antimony" translates *pukh*, a black paint for the eyes and the mortar of stones.
- **54:12** "Your pinnacles" translates *shimshotayikh*, "agate" translates *kadkod*, and "carbuncles" translates *eqdach*. The names of the stones are uncertain. See Revelation 21:18-21.
- **54:13** "Taught by the LORD" translates *limmudei YHWH*, and is quoted in John 6:45. "The peace of your children" translates *shelom banayikh*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **54:14** "In righteousness" translates *bi-tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Oppression" translates *osheq*.
- **54:15** "Stirs up strife" translates *gor yagur*, and "it is not from me" translates *efes me-ittiy*. The verse is hard.
- **54:16** "The smith" translates *charash*, and "the ravager" translates *mashchit*.
- **54:17** "You will refute" translates *tarshi'i*. "Their vindication from me" translates *tsidqatam me-ittiy*, in which *tsidqah* is the word *tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **55:1** "Come to the waters" translates *lekhu la-mayim*, and "buy" translates *shiv'ru*. "Without money" translates *be-lo khasef*.
- **55:2** "Spend your money" translates *tishqelu-khesef*, "your labour" translates *yegi'akhem*, and "rich food" translates *deshen*.
- **55:3** "Your soul" translates *nafshekhem*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "An everlasting covenant" translates *berit olam*. See entry H-BERIT. "The steadfast, sure love shown to David" translates *chasdei David ha-ne'emanim*, which may be read "the faithful acts of steadfast love for David". See entry H-HESED. Acts 13:34 quotes the Greek form ("the holy and sure blessings of David").
- **55:4** "A witness" translates *ed*, "a leader" translates *nagid*, and "a commander" translates *metsavveh*.
- **55:5** "He has glorified you" translates *pe'arekha*.
- **55:6** "While he may be found" translates *be-himmatse'o*.
- **55:7** "The unrighteous" translates *ish aven*. "Let him return" translates *ve-yashov*. See entry H-SHUV. "That he may have compassion" translates *ve-yerachamehu*, and "he will abundantly pardon" translates *yarbeh lis'loach*.
- **55:10** "Do not return there" translates *shamma lo yashuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **55:11** "My word" translates *devari*, "empty" translates *reqam*, and "succeed" translates *hitslicha*.
- **55:12** "Be led forth in peace" translates *be-shalom tuvalun*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **55:13** "The thorn" translates *na'atsuts*, "the brier" translates *sirpad*, "the cypress" translates *berosh*, and "the myrtle" translates *hadas*. "A name for the LORD" translates *le-YHWH le-shem*, and "a sign" translates *le-ot*.
- **56:1** "Keep justice" translates *shimru mishpat*. "Righteousness" translates *tsedaqah*, and "my righteousness" translates *tsidqati*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **56:2** "The human being" translates *ben-adam*, "holds fast" translates *yachazik*, and "the Sabbath" translates *shabbat*.
- **56:3** "The foreigner who has joined himself" translates *ben ha-nekhar ha-nilveh*, and "the eunuch" translates *ha-saris*. "A dry tree" translates *ets yavesh*. The verses 3 to 8 are in contrast with Deuteronomy 23:1-8.
- **56:4** "My covenant" translates *beriti*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **56:5** "A monument and a name" translates *yad va-shem*, literally "a hand and a name".
- **56:7** "My holy mountain" translates *har qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH. "A house of prayer for all peoples" translates *beit tefillah le-khol ha-ammim*, and is quoted in Matthew 21:13, Mark 11:17 and Luke 19:46.
- **56:8** "Who gathers the outcasts of Israel" translates *me'assef nidchei Yisra'el*.
- **56:10** "Silent dogs" translates *kelavim illemim*, and "dreaming" translates *hozim*, a rare word. The verses 9 to 12 are hard.
- **56:11** "Have a mighty appetite" translates *azzei nefesh*, "strong of soul", an idiom of hunger.
- **57:1** "The devout" translates *anshei chesed*, "people of steadfast love". See entry H-HESED.
- **57:2** "Peace" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Their uprightness" translates *nekhocho*.
- **57:3** "The sorceress" translates *onenah*.
- **57:5** "Who burn with lust" translates *ha-nechamim*, "oaks" translates *elim*, and "who slaughter your children" translates *shochatei ha-yeladim*.
- **57:6** "The smooth stones of the valley" translates *challeqei-nachal*, which is a play on words with "your portion" (*chelqekh*). "Shall I relent" translates *ha-al-eleh enachem*, in which the verb is *nacham* (see entry H-NACHAM).
- **57:8** "Your memorial" translates *zikronekh*. "A covenant" translates *berit*. See entry H-BERIT. "You have looked on nakedness" translates *yad chazit*, literally "you saw a hand", which is hard.
- **57:9** "To the king" translates *la-melekh*, which may also be read "to Molech". "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL.
- **57:10** "New life for your strength" translates *chayyat yadekh*, which is hard.
- **57:13** "Your collection of idols" translates *qibbutsayikh*. "A breath" translates *hevel*, in the same sense as in Ecclesiastes 1:2.
- **57:14** "Obstruction" translates *michshol*.
- **57:15** "The One who is high and lifted up" translates *ram ve-nissa*, as in 6:1. "Who inhabits eternity" translates *shokhen ad*. "Holy" translates *qadosh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "A contrite and lowly spirit" translates *dakka u-shefal ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "To revive" translates *le-hachayot*.
- **57:16** "The spirit" translates *ruach*, and "the breath of life" translates *neshamot*, a plural form.
- **57:17** "His unjust gain" translates *betsa*, and "backsliding" translates *shovav*, from *shuv*.
- **57:18** "Comfort" translates *nichumim*. See entry H-NACHAM. "His mourners" translates *avelav*.
- **57:19** "The fruit of the lips" translates *nov sefatayim*. "Peace, peace" translates *shalom shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **57:20** "The tossing sea" translates *ke-yam nigrash*.
- **57:21** The verse is the same as 48:22.
- **58:1** "A trumpet" translates *shofar*.
- **58:2** "Righteous judgements" translates *mishpetei tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **58:3** "Humbled ourselves" translates *initu nafshenu*, literally "afflicted our souls". See entry H-NEPHESH. "Your own pleasure" translates *cheifets*, and "oppress" translates *tiggosu*.
- **58:4** "A wicked fist" translates *be-egrof resha*.
- **58:5** "A reed" translates *agmon*.
- **58:6** "The bonds of wickedness" translates *chartsubbot resha*, and "the straps of the yoke" translates *agguddot mota*.
- **58:7** "The homeless poor" translates *aniyyim merudim*, and "your own flesh" translates *mi-besarkha*.
- **58:8** "Will break forth" translates *yibbaqa*, and "your healing" translates *arukhatkha*. "Your righteousness" translates *tsidqekha*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Your rear guard" translates *ya'asfekha*, as in 52:12.
- **58:9** "The pointing of the finger" translates *shelach etsba*, and "speaking wickedness" translates *dabber aven*.
- **58:10** "If you pour yourself out" translates *ve-tafeq la-ra'ev nafshekha*, literally "if you bring out your soul". "The desire of the afflicted" translates *nefesh na'anah*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **58:11** "Your desire" translates *nafshekha*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Scorched places" translates *tsachtsachot*, and "a watered garden" translates *gan raveh*.
- **58:12** "The repairer of the breach" translates *gonder perets*, and "the restorer of streets" translates *meshovev netivot*, in which the first word is from *shuv*.
- **58:13** "If you turn back your foot" translates *im-tashiv mi-shabbat raglekha*, from *shuv*. "Honourable" translates *mekhubbad*.
- **58:14** "The heights of the earth" translates *bamotei arets*.
- **59:1** "Is not shortened" translates *lo-qatsera*, and "dull" translates *kavda*.
- **59:2** "A separation" translates *mavdilim*, and "have hidden" translates *histiru*.
- **59:3** "Are defiled" translates *ge'alu*, and "mutters" translates *tehgeh*.
- **59:4** "Enters suit justly" translates *qore be-tsedeq*, "empty pleas" translates *tohu*, "mischief" translates *amal*, and "iniquity" translates *aven*.
- **59:5** "Adders' eggs" translates *bitsei tsif'oni*, "the spider's web" translates *qurei akavish*, and "a viper" translates *efeh*.
- **59:7** "Their feet run to evil" translates *raglehem la-ra yarutsu*, as in Proverbs 1:16. The verses 7 and 8 are quoted in Romans 3:15-17 in the Greek form.
- **59:8** "The way of peace" translates *derekh shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "They have made their roads crooked" translates *netivotehem iqqeshu lahem*.
- **59:9** "Righteousness" translates *tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **59:10** "We grope" translates *negashshesha*, and "in full vigour" translates *ba-ashmannim*, a rare word.
- **59:11** "Like bears" translates *ka-dubbim*, and "like doves" translates *ka-yonim*.
- **59:13** "Denying" translates *kachesh*, "oppression" translates *osheq*, and "revolt" translates *sarah*.
- **59:14** "The public squares" translates *ba-rechov*.
- **59:15** "Makes himself a prey" translates *mishtolel*.
- **59:16** "To intercede" translates *mafgia*. "His righteousness" translates *tsidqato*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The verse is as in 63:5.
- **59:17** "A breastplate" translates *shiryon*, and "a helmet of salvation" translates *kova yeshu'ah*. It is echoed in Ephesians 6:14-17 and 1 Thessalonians 5:8.
- **59:18** "Repayment" translates *gemul*, and "the coastlands" translates *iyyim*.
- **59:19** "The wind of the LORD drives" translates *ruach YHWH nesesah bo*, which is hard. The word *ruach* may mean "wind", "breath" or "Spirit", and the verb may be read "drives" or "lifts a banner against it". See entry H-RUACH.
- **59:20** "A Redeemer" translates *go'el*. See entry H-GOEL. "To Zion" translates *le-Tsiyyon*. The verse is quoted in Romans 11:26-27 in the Greek form, in which the words are "from Zion". "Who turn from transgression" translates *shavei fesha*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **59:21** "My covenant" translates *beriti*. See entry H-BERIT. "My Spirit that is on you" translates *ruchi asher alekha*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **60:1** "Arise, shine" translates *qumi ori*, and "has risen" translates *zarach*.
- **60:2** "Thick darkness" translates *arafel*.
- **60:4** "Will be carried on the hip" translates *al-tsad te'amanah*.
- **60:5** "Be radiant" translates *ve-nahartt*, "the abundance of the sea" translates *hamon yam*, and "the wealth" translates *chel*.
- **60:6** "The young camels" translates *bikhrei*, and "frankincense" translates *levonah*.
- **60:7** "Kedar" and "Nebaioth" are tribes of the Arabian desert.
- **60:8** "To their windows" translates *el-arubboteihem*.
- **60:9** "The Holy One of Israel" translates *qedosh Yisra'el*. See entry H-QADOSH. "He has made you beautiful" translates *pe'arakh*.
- **60:10** "In my favour" translates *bi-retsoni*, and "I have had mercy" translates *richamtikh*.
- **60:14** "The City of the LORD" translates *ir YHWH*.
- **60:15** "Majestic" translates *ge'on*.
- **60:16** "Your Redeemer" translates *go'alekh*. See entry H-GOEL. "The Mighty One of Jacob" translates *avir Ya'aqov*.
- **60:17** "Your overseers" translates *pequddatekh*, and "your taskmasters" translates *nogsayikh*. "Peace" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Righteousness" translates *tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **60:18** "Salvation" translates *yeshu'ah*, and "Praise" translates *tehillah*.
- **60:19** The verses 19 and 20 are echoed in Revelation 21:23 and 22:5.
- **60:21** "Righteous" translates *tsaddiqim*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "The branch of my planting" translates *netser matta'ai*.
- **60:22** "A clan" translates *eleph*, and "in its time I will hasten it" translates *be-ittah achishennah*.
- **61:1** "The Spirit of the Lord GOD" translates *ruach Adonai YHWH*. See entry H-RUACH. "Has anointed me" translates *mashach YHWH oti*. See entry H-MASHIACH. "To bring good news" translates *le-vasser*, "the poor" translates *anavim*, and "the brokenhearted" translates *nishberei lev*. "Liberty" translates *deror*. "The opening of the prison" translates *pequach-qoach*, a rare word that may be read "the release of the bound". The Greek has "recovery of sight for the blind", and Luke 4:18-19 quotes that form.
- **61:2** "The year of the LORD's favour" translates *shenat ratson la-YHWH*, and "the day of vengeance of our God" translates *yom naqam le-Eloheinu*. It is rendered as written. In Luke 4:19 the quotation stops after the year of favour. "To comfort" translates *le-nachem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **61:3** "A beautiful headdress" translates *pe'er*, and "ashes" translates *efer*. "A faint spirit" translates *ruach kehah*. See entry H-RUACH. "Oaks of righteousness" translates *eilei ha-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **61:6** "Priests of the LORD" translates *kohanei YHWH*, and "ministers" translates *meshartei*. "You will boast" translates *titvammaru*, a rare word.
- **61:7** "A double portion" translates *mishneh*.
- **61:8** "Robbery and wrong" follows the Dead Sea scroll and the versions (*gazel be-avlah*). The Hebrew text has *gazel be-olah*, "robbery with a burnt offering". The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar. "An everlasting covenant" translates *berit olam*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **61:10** "My soul" translates *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "The robe of righteousness" translates *me'il tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Decks himself like a priest" translates *yekhahen*.
- **61:11** "Righteousness and praise" translates *tsedaqah ve-tehillah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **62:1** "Her righteousness" translates *tsidqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Brightness" translates *nogah*, and "a burning torch" translates *lappid*.
- **62:2** "A new name" translates *shem chadash*.
- **62:3** "A crown of beauty" translates *atarat tif'eret*, and "a royal diadem" translates *tsenif melukhah*.
- **62:4** "Forsaken" translates *Azuvah*, "Desolate" translates *Shemamah*, "My Delight Is in Her" translates *Chefts-i-vah* (Hephzibah), and "Married" translates *Beulah*.
- **62:5** "So your sons will marry you" translates *yivalukh banayikh*, in which the Hebrew may also be read "so your Builder will marry you" (*bonayikh*). "A young man ... a young woman" translates *bachur ... betulah*.
- **62:6** "Watchmen" translates *shomerim*, and "you who put the LORD in remembrance" translates *ha-mazkirim et-YHWH*.
- **62:10** "The highway" translates *mesillah*, and "a signal" translates *nes*.
- **62:11** "His reward is with him" is as in 40:10.
- **62:12** "The Holy People" translates *am ha-qodesh*. See entry H-QADOSH. "The Redeemed of the LORD" translates *ge'ulei YHWH*. See entry H-GOEL. "Sought Out" translates *Derushah*, and "A City Not Forsaken" translates *Ir lo ne'ezavah*.
- **63:1** "Crimsoned" translates *chamots*, which is hard. "Splendid" translates *hadur*, and "marching" translates *tso'eh*. "Speaking in righteousness" translates *medabber bi-tsedaqah*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Mighty to save" translates *rav le-hoshia*.
- **63:2** "The winepress" translates *gat*.
- **63:3** "I have trodden the winepress alone" translates *purah dorakhti levaddi*. "Lifeblood" translates *nitsham*, and "stained" translates *ga'alti*, a word that has a sound like *go'el* ("redeemer"). The passage 63:1-6 is rendered as written.
- **63:4** "The day of vengeance" translates *yom naqam*, and "my year of redemption" translates *shenat ge'ulai*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **63:5** The verse is as in 59:16.
- **63:6** "I made them drunk" translates *va-ashakkerem*.
- **63:7** "The steadfast love of the LORD" translates *chasdei YHWH*. See entry H-HESED.
- **63:8** "Their Saviour" translates *moshia*.
- **63:9** "In all their affliction he was afflicted" follows the reading tradition and the versions (*lo tsar*, "to him was affliction"). The written text has *lo*, "not", so that it reads "in all their affliction he was not afflicted". "The angel of his presence" translates *mal'akh panav*. "He redeemed them" translates *ge'alam*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **63:10** "Grieved" translates *itstsevu*. "His holy Spirit" translates *ruach qodsho*. See entries H-RUACH and H-QADOSH. The phrase is used in the Hebrew Bible here and in Psalm 51:11 only. The footnote does not decide its later use.
- **63:11** "Then he remembered the days of old, of Moses and his people" translates *va-yizkor yemei-olam Moshe ammo*, which is hard, and the subject may be "he" or "his people". "The shepherds of his flock" translates *ro'ei tso'no*. The Dead Sea scroll and the Greek have the singular, "the shepherd", which may refer to Moses.
- **63:12** "His glorious arm" translates *zero'a tif'arto*, and "an everlasting name" translates *shem olam*.
- **63:13** "The depths" translates *tehomot*.
- **63:14** "Livestock" translates *behemah*. "The Spirit of the LORD" translates *ruach YHWH*. See entry H-RUACH. "Gave them rest" translates *tenichennu*.
- **63:15** "Your holy and beautiful habitation" translates *mi-zevul qodshekha ve-tif'artekha*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Your zeal" translates *qin'atekha*, "the stirring of your inner parts" translates *hamon me'ekha*, and "your compassion" translates *rachamekha*.
- **63:16** "Our Father" translates *avinu*. "Our Redeemer from of old" translates *go'alenu me-olam*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **63:17** "Make us wander" translates *tatenu*, and "harden our heart" translates *taqshiach libbenu*. "Return" translates *shuv*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **63:18** "Your holy people" translates *am qodshekha*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Held possession for a little while" translates *le-mitsar yarashu*, which is hard.
- **63:19** The verse is hard. In the Hebrew text it is divided, and the first part is the end of 63:19, and the second part begins chapter 64. The English verse numbers of chapter 64 are one higher than the Hebrew verse numbers (English 64:1-12 is Hebrew 63:19 to 64:11).
- **64:1** "Rend the heavens" translates *qarata shamayim*.
- **64:2** "Brushwood" translates *hamasim*, a rare word.
- **64:3** "Awesome things" translates *nora'ot*.
- **64:4** "No eye has seen a God besides you" translates *ayin lo ra'atah Elohim zulatekha*. It is echoed in 1 Corinthians 2:9.
- **64:5** "Works righteousness" translates *oseh tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "In our sins we have been a long time, and will we be saved" is hard in the Hebrew.
- **64:6** "Like one who is unclean" translates *ka-tame*, "a polluted garment" translates *beged iddim*, and "our righteous deeds" translates *tsidqotenu*. "We all fade" translates *nabbel*.
- **64:7** "Rouses himself" translates *mit'orer*, and "have made us melt" translates *temugenu*.
- **64:8** "Our potter" translates *yotsrenu*.
- **64:10** "Your holy cities" translates *arei qodshekha*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **64:11** "Our holy and beautiful house" translates *beit qodshenu ve-tif'artenu*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Our pleasant places" translates *machamaddenu*.
- **64:12** "Will you restrain yourself" translates *ha-al-eleh tit'appaq*.
- **65:1** "I was ready to be sought" translates *nidrashti*, "to be found" translates *nimtseti*, and "here I am" translates *hinneni*. The verses 1 and 2 are quoted in Romans 10:20-21 in the Greek form.
- **65:2** "I spread out my hands" translates *paraskhti yadai*, and "a rebellious people" translates *am sorer*.
- **65:3** "Gardens" translates *gannot*, and "bricks" translates *levenim*.
- **65:4** "Tombs" translates *qevarim*, "secret places" translates *netsurim*, "pig's flesh" translates *basar ha-chazir*, and "tainted" translates *pigul*.
- **65:5** "I am too holy for you" translates *ki qedashtikha*. See entry H-QADOSH. The words may also be read "lest I make you holy" (by touch).
- **65:6** "It is written before me" translates *ketuvah lefanai*.
- **65:8** "The new wine" translates *tirosh*, and "the cluster" translates *eshkol*.
- **65:10** "The Valley of Achor" translates *Emeq Akhor*, as in Joshua 7:24-26 and Hosea 2:15.
- **65:11** "My holy mountain" translates *har qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Fortune" translates *Gad*, and "Destiny" translates *Meni*, the names of gods of fortune and fate. "Mixed wine" translates *mimsakh*.
- **65:12** "I will destine you" translates *u-manitti*, in a play on words with *Meni*.
- **65:14** "Breaking of spirit" translates *shever ruach*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **65:15** "A curse" translates *shevu'ah*, which means an oath-formula.
- **65:16** "The God of truth" translates *Elohei amen*, in which *amen* means "truth" or "faithfulness".
- **65:17** "I create new heavens and a new earth" translates *bore shamayim chadashim ve-arets chadashah*. See also 66:22, 2 Peter 3:13 and Revelation 21:1. The footnote does not comment on the later use.
- **65:19** "The sound of weeping and the cry of distress" is echoed in Revelation 21:4.
- **65:20** "An infant who lives but a few days" translates *ol yamim*. "The young will die a hundred years old" translates *ha-na'ar ben-me'ah shanah yamut*, and "will be accursed" translates *yequlal*. The verse is hard.
- **65:23** "For calamity" translates *la-vehalah*.
- **65:25** "The wolf and the lamb will graze together" is as in 11:6-9, with the words "dust will be the serpent's food" added.
- **66:1** "Heaven is my throne" is quoted in Acts 7:49-50 in the Greek form.
- **66:2** "Humble and contrite in spirit" translates *ani u-nekheh ruach*. See entry H-RUACH. "Trembles at my word" translates *chared al-devari*.
- **66:3** The lines are juxtaposed in the Hebrew, and the word "like" is not in the text, so that they may be read "one who slaughters an ox, one who strikes down a person" (the same persons do both) or with "like" as in the WABV text. "Strikes down a person" translates *makkeh ish*, "breaks a dog's neck" translates *oref kelev*, "a memorial offering" translates *mazkir levonah*, and "blesses an idol" translates *mevarekh aven*. "Their soul" translates *nafsham*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **66:5** "You who tremble at his word" translates *ha-charedim el-devaro*. "Let the LORD be glorified" translates *yikhbad YHWH*. The verse is hard.
- **66:6** "An uproar" translates *qol sha'on*, and "the temple" translates *heikhal*.
- **66:9** "Shall I bring to the point of birth" translates *ha-ani asbir*, and "shut the womb" translates *he'atsarti*.
- **66:11** "Her consoling breast" translates *shod tanchumeha*. See entry H-NACHAM. "That you may drink deeply" translates *tammotsu*, which is hard.
- **66:12** "Peace" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "You will be carried on her hip" translates *al-tsad tinnase'u*.
- **66:13** "Comforts" translates *yenachamenu*, and "I will comfort" translates *anokhi anachemkhem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **66:14** "Your bones will flourish" translates *atsmotekhem ka-deshe tifrachna*, and "his indignation" translates *za'am*.
- **66:15** "His rebuke" translates *ge'aratto*.
- **66:17** "Those who sanctify and purify themselves" translates *ha-mitqaddeshim ve-ha-mittaharim*. "Following one in the midst" translates *achar achat ba-tavekh*, in which the written text has the feminine "one" and the reading tradition the masculine, and the phrase is hard. "Mice" translates *ha-akhbar*, and "the abomination" translates *ha-sheqets*.
- **66:18** "To gather" translates *le-qabbets*.
- **66:19** "Put" follows the Greek and the Latin. The Hebrew text has *Pul*. The WABV text departs from the Hebrew here, and is flagged for the scholar. "Survivors" translates *peletim*, and "who draw the bow" translates *moshekhei qeshet*. "Tubal and Javan" are places to the north and west.
- **66:20** "An offering" translates *minchah*, "litters" translates *tsabbim*, and "dromedaries" translates *kirkarot*. "My holy mountain" translates *har qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH. "A clean vessel" translates *kli tahor*.
- **66:21** "For priests and for Levites" translates *la-kohanim la-leviyyim*.
- **66:22** "The new heavens and the new earth" translates *ha-shamayim ha-chadashim ve-ha-arets ha-chadashah*. See also 65:17.
- **66:23** "All flesh will come to worship before me" translates *yavo kol-basar le-hishtachavot lefanai*.
- **66:24** "The dead bodies" translates *pigrei*. "Their worm will not die, their fire will not be quenched" translates *tola'tam lo tamut ve-esham lo tikhbeh*. It is rendered as written, with no softening. It is echoed in Mark 9:48 in the Greek form. "An abhorrence" translates *dera'on*, a rare word that is also used in Daniel 12:2.

| Verse | References | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1:1-9 | 2 Kings 15:1-20:21; 2 Chronicles 26:1-32:33; Deuteronomy 32:1; Jeremiah 8:7; Genesis 19:24-25; Romans 9:29 | Parallel and allusion: the title, the rebellious children, and the few survivors |
| 1:10-20 | Amos 5:21-24; Hosea 6:6; Micah 6:6-8; Psalm 50:7-15; Jeremiah 7:21-23; Isaiah 58:6-7; Micah 3:4 | Parallel: sacrifice without justice, and "come now, let us reason together" |
| 1:21-31 | Hosea 2:2-13; Jeremiah 2:20; Ezekiel 16:15; Isaiah 48:10; Jeremiah 6:27-30; Isaiah 57:5 | Parallel: the faithful city that became a prostitute, and the refining |
| 2:1-5 | Micah 4:1-3; Joel 3:10; Zechariah 8:20-23; Psalm 46:9; Luke 1:79 | Parallel: the mountain of the LORD and the swords turned into ploughshares |
| 2:6-22 | Isaiah 5:15-16; 10:33-34; 13:6-13; Revelation 6:15-17; Hosea 10:8; Zechariah 14:1-5 | Parallel and allusion: the day of the LORD and the idols |
| 3:1-15 | Isaiah 5:13; 9:14-17; Ecclesiastes 10:16; Micah 3:1-3; Psalm 82:1-8; Isaiah 1:23 | Parallel: the removal of support and leaders, and the case against the elders |
| 3:16-4:1 | Amos 4:1-3; Isaiah 32:9-14; Ezekiel 16:49-50; Deuteronomy 28:53-57 | Parallel: the daughters of Zion and their finery |
| 4:2-6 | Jeremiah 23:5; 33:15; Zechariah 3:8; 6:12; Exodus 13:21-22; 40:34-38; Isaiah 60:19; Psalm 91:1 | Parallel and allusion: the branch of the LORD and the cloud over Zion |
| 5:1-7 | Psalm 80:8-16; Jeremiah 2:21; Ezekiel 15:1-8; Matthew 21:33-43; Mark 12:1-12; John 15:1-6; Hosea 10:1 | Parallel and allusion: the song of the vineyard |
| 5:8-24 | Micah 2:1-2; Amos 6:1-6; Isaiah 22:12-13; Amos 5:18-20; Isaiah 10:1-2; Proverbs 17:15; Psalm 50:16-22 | Parallel: the six woes |
| 5:25-30 | Isaiah 9:12, 17, 21; 10:4; 7:18-20; Deuteronomy 28:49-52; Jeremiah 5:15-17; Habakkuk 1:6-9 | Parallel and allusion: the hand stretched out and the nation from afar |
| 6:1-13 | 1 Kings 22:19; Exodus 33:20; Ezekiel 1:4-28; Revelation 4:8; John 12:41; Matthew 13:14-15; Acts 28:25-27; Jeremiah 1:4-10 | Parallel and allusion: the call, the holy, holy, holy and the hardening |
| 7:1-9 | 2 Kings 16:5-9; 2 Chronicles 28:5-8; Isaiah 36:2; 2 Chronicles 20:20; Habakkuk 2:4 | Parallel: the war of Syria and Ephraim against Judah |
| 7:10-25 | Matthew 1:22-23; Isaiah 8:8, 10; 9:6-7; Deuteronomy 6:16; Genesis 24:43; Isaiah 5:6; 36:1 | Parallel and allusion: the sign of Immanuel and the day of Assyria |
| 8:1-10 | Isaiah 7:14-16; 10:5-12; 7:3; 2 Kings 16:10-16; Psalm 46:7, 11; Joel 3:9-11 | Parallel and allusion: Maher-shalal-hash-baz and the waters of the River |
| 8:11-22 | Romans 9:33; 1 Peter 2:6-8; Hebrews 2:13; Leviticus 19:31; 20:6; Deuteronomy 18:10-12; Isaiah 29:10-12 | Parallel and allusion: the stone of offence, the testimony and the teaching, and the mediums |
| 9:1-7 | Matthew 4:12-16; 2 Kings 15:29; Judges 7:1-25; Luke 1:32-33; Isaiah 7:14; 11:1-5; Micah 5:2-5; Ephesians 2:14-17 | Parallel and allusion: the great light and the child born to us |
| 9:8-21 | Isaiah 5:25; 10:4; 2 Kings 17:1-23; Hosea 7:10; 13:1-9; Isaiah 3:12; Micah 7:2-6 | Parallel: the hand stretched out still and the fall of Ephraim |
| 10:1-19 | Isaiah 5:8, 23; 37:12-13; 2 Kings 18:33-35; Jeremiah 51:20-23; Isaiah 14:24-27; Habakkuk 1:6-11; 2:6-8 | Parallel and allusion: the woe on Assyria, the rod of my anger and the boast of the axe |
| 10:20-34 | Isaiah 7:3; 11:11, 16; Romans 9:27-28; Judges 7:25; Exodus 14:16; 2 Kings 19:35-37; Isaiah 37:36-38 | Parallel and allusion: the remnant that will return and the march on Jerusalem |
| 11:1-10 | Jeremiah 23:5-6; Zechariah 6:12; Luke 3:21-22; Matthew 3:16; 2 Thessalonians 2:8; Isaiah 65:25; Hosea 2:18; Habakkuk 2:14; Romans 15:12 | Parallel and allusion: the shoot from the stump of Jesse and the peaceable kingdom |
| 11:11-16 | Isaiah 27:12-13; 43:5-6; 49:22; Zechariah 10:8-12; Exodus 14:21-29; Hosea 11:10-11; Ezekiel 37:15-23 | Parallel and allusion: the second gathering and the highway from Assyria |
| 12:1-6 | Exodus 15:2; Psalm 118:14, 21; John 7:37-38; Psalm 105:1; Isaiah 25:9; Zephaniah 3:14-17 | Parallel and allusion: the song of thanks and the wells of salvation |
| 13:1-22 | Isaiah 21:1-10; 47:1-15; Jeremiah 50:1-51:64; Genesis 19:24-25; Psalm 137:8-9; Joel 2:1-11; Revelation 18:1-24 | Parallel and allusion: the oracle on Babylon and the day of the LORD |
| 14:1-23 | Ezekiel 28:1-19; 31:1-18; Luke 10:15, 18; Revelation 9:1; Isaiah 47:8; Daniel 4:30; Psalm 82:7 | Parallel and allusion: the taunt over the king of Babylon, Sheol and the Day Star |
| 14:24-32 | Isaiah 10:5-34; 37:36-38; 9:12; 2 Kings 18:8; Jeremiah 47:1-7; Psalm 87:1-5 | Parallel: the LORD's plan against Assyria and the oracle on Philistia |
| 15:1-9 | Jeremiah 48:1-47; Numbers 21:28-30; Amos 2:1-3; Ezekiel 25:8-11; Zephaniah 2:8-11 | Parallel: the oracle on Moab and the lament |
| 16:1-14 | Jeremiah 48:29-34; 2 Kings 3:4-5; Isaiah 9:6-7; 11:1-5; Amos 9:11; 2 Samuel 7:16 | Parallel and allusion: the fugitives of Moab, the throne in steadfast love, and the pride of Moab |
| 17:1-14 | Jeremiah 49:23-27; Amos 1:3-5; 2 Kings 16:9; Isaiah 7:8; 8:4; 10:20-23; Psalm 65:7; Isaiah 29:5-8 | Parallel and allusion: the oracle on Damascus, the gleanings and the roaring nations |
| 18:1-7 | Psalm 68:31; Zephaniah 3:10; Isaiah 20:1-6; 37:9; 45:14; Ezekiel 30:4-9 | Parallel: the oracle on Cush and the gifts to Mount Zion |
| 19:1-15 | Jeremiah 46:1-26; Ezekiel 29:1-32:32; Exodus 7:14-12:36; Psalm 18:10; Isaiah 9:14-15; 30:1-5 | Parallel and allusion: the oracle on Egypt, the drying of the Nile and the spirit of confusion |
| 19:16-25 | Zephaniah 3:9; Hosea 6:1; Isaiah 11:16; 27:13; 56:6-8; Psalm 87:4; Zechariah 8:20-23; Deuteronomy 32:9 | Parallel and allusion: Egypt, Assyria and Israel as a blessing |
| 20:1-6 | 2 Kings 18:17; Isaiah 30:1-5; 31:1-3; Ezekiel 4:1-17; Hosea 1:2-9; Jeremiah 13:1-11 | Parallel: the prophet's sign act against Egypt and Cush |
| 21:1-10 | Isaiah 13:1-22; 47:1-15; Jeremiah 51:8; Daniel 5:1-31; Revelation 14:8; 18:2; Habakkuk 2:1-3 | Parallel and allusion: the fall of Babylon and the watchman |
| 21:11-17 | Jeremiah 49:7-22, 28-33; Genesis 25:3, 13; Isaiah 16:14; Psalm 130:6; Ezekiel 25:12-14 | Parallel: the oracles on Dumah and Arabia |
| 22:1-14 | 2 Kings 20:20; 2 Chronicles 32:2-5, 30; Nehemiah 3:16; 1 Corinthians 15:32; Isaiah 5:11-12; Luke 12:19-20 | Parallel and allusion: the valley of vision, the city that did not look to the LORD |
| 22:15-25 | 2 Kings 18:18, 26, 37; Isaiah 36:3, 22; 37:2; Revelation 3:7; Job 12:14; Matthew 16:19 | Parallel and allusion: Shebna, Eliakim and the key of the house of David |
| 23:1-18 | Ezekiel 26:1-28:19; Jeremiah 25:11-12; 47:4; Amos 1:9-10; Zechariah 9:2-4; Revelation 18:11-19; Zechariah 14:20-21 | Parallel and allusion: the oracle on Tyre and the seventy years |
| 24:1-13 | Genesis 9:16; Jeremiah 4:23-28; Hosea 4:1-3; Isaiah 33:8-9; Leviticus 18:24-28; Isaiah 17:6; Joel 1:10-12 | Parallel and allusion: the earth laid waste and the everlasting covenant broken |
| 24:14-23 | Genesis 7:11; Jeremiah 48:43-44; Amos 5:19; Isaiah 2:19-21; 13:10-13; Revelation 6:12-17; Zechariah 14:9 | Parallel and allusion: terror, the pit and the snare, and the LORD reigning on Zion |
